| [R22e. Quick clips: a shorter first clause](#r22e-quick-clips-a-shorter-first-clause-simple--item-12--done-in-pr-53) | Cuts the first clause shorter, so the first words are heard sooner. | Done in [PR #53](https://github.com/larry94555/ImageSkinForLLM/pull/53) |
| [R22f. Quick clips: a fast clone of the person's voice](#r22f-quick-clips-a-fast-clone-of-the-persons-voice-medium--items-4-12--done-in-pr-55) | A fast clone of the person's voice with Pocket TTS. | Done in [PR #55](https://github.com/larry94555/ImageSkinForLLM/pull/55) |
| [R22g. Quick clips: the performance cores](#r22g-quick-clips-the-performance-cores-simple--item-12--done-in-pr-56) | Keeps the app on a laptop's performance cores. | Done in [PR #56](https://github.com/larry94555/ImageSkinForLLM/pull/56) |
| [R23. Idle video and smooth joins](#r23-idle-video-and-smooth-joins-medium--item-17--done-in-pr-61) | The person blinks and moves slightly between replies, with smooth joins between clips. | Done in [PR #61](https://github.com/larry94555/ImageSkinForLLM/pull/61) |

### R20. Streaming LLM text and sentence splitting (Simple) · item 12 · Done in [PR #46](https://github.com/larry94555/ImageSkinForLLM/pull/46)
- The LLM client streams text; a splitter turns it into sentences as they complete, handling abbreviations, numbers and decimals.
- **Can show:** in the logs, sentences arriving one at a time while the LLM is still writing.
- **Built:** `LlmClient.stream()` asks with `"stream": true` and reads the server-sent events; `sentences.SentenceSplitter` hands back each sentence once the next one starts. A sentence ends at ".", "!", "?" or an ellipsis followed by a space, at a blank line, and at the end of a heading or list item. It doesn't end after an abbreviation (Dr., e.g.), an initial (J.), a list number (1.), a dotted abbreviation (U.S.) or a full stop followed by a lowercase word or a digit; code blocks stay whole. The log shows `Reply started` and `Reply sentence ready` with the time since the prompt. The summary is still asked for in one piece.

### R21. Sentence-by-sentence rendering on the server (Medium) · item 12 · Done in [PR #47](https://github.com/larry94555/ImageSkinForLLM/pull/47)
- Voices and animates each sentence while later ones are still generating, keeping them in order.
- Logs time from the LLM's first words to the first sentence being ready.
- **Can show:** in the logs, the first sentence's video is ready before the LLM has finished the reply, with the measured time.
- **Built:** each streamed sentence goes to `SentenceClips`, which cleans it for speech and renders its clip on a background thread, in order, while the LLM writes the next one; the engines render one clip at a time. R21 joined the clips into one video per reply with ffmpeg; R22 replaced that by playing the clips themselves. A reply that fails drops its clips. The log shows `Sentence clip ready` with `render_ms` and `since_first_words_ms`. Measured with Gemma 3 4B, Kokoro and the photoreal engine on a 4-core cloud CPU: the first sentence's clip was ready 6.5 s after the LLM's first words, while the LLM was still writing (its reply took 11.8 s); the LLM and the rendering share the CPU, so each slows the other.

### R22. Ordered playback in the browser (Medium) · item 12 · Done in [PR #48](https://github.com/larry94555/ImageSkinForLLM/pull/48)
- The browser receives sentence clips as they are ready and plays them in order.
- **Can show:** the video starts on the first sentence instead of waiting for the whole reply.
- **Built:** the chat page gives each prompt a reply id and asks `GET /api/chat/clips/{id}` every 0.3 s for the clips ready so far; it plays them one after another, starting before the reply text arrives. An LLM that doesn't stream still gets one video per reply. Measured with Gemma 3 4B, Kokoro and the photoreal engine on a 4-core cloud CPU: the first clip played 4.9 s after Send, while the reply text took 12.6 s. That needed the LLM and the engines to split the cores (llama-server `--threads 2`, `OMP_NUM_THREADS=2` for imageskin); with both using every core the first clip took 13.5 s, after the text. Later clips render a little slower than they play, so there are short pauses between sentences until Milestone 5. Review fixes: the reply says whether it was `streamed`, so a reply whose clips a newer prompt took (from another tab, say) is not spoken again as one video; a clip that fails leaves no file behind; clips that can't be removed (a browser still has one open, as on Windows) are logged and removed with the next reply; a clip failing before the reply is in is reported with it, not as an unhandled rejection.

### R22a. Quick clips: shared cores, warm start and the timing readout (Medium) · item 12 · Done in [PR #49](https://github.com/larry94555/ImageSkinForLLM/pull/49)
- Added after Larry's test of R22 (2026-10-10): the gap between a sentence's text arriving and the person speaking it must be 1 to 2 seconds, on a 4-core CPU with no graphics card, and the page must show how long it took. This is make or break for the project.
- Measured first, with Gemma 3 4B in llama-server and Kokoro on a 4-core CPU: with both using every core (their defaults), Kokoro took 14.5 s to speak a 3-second sentence while the LLM wrote, and the LLM fell from 8.4 to 0.3 tokens a second, because each one's threads spin waiting for cores the other holds. With 2 threads each: 1.3 s, and the LLM kept 4.7 tokens a second.
- While the LLM is busy (writing, or summarizing the conversation after a reply), the engines use half the cores (PyTorch and OpenCV threads, applied on the thread that runs them, as PyTorch keeps a count per thread), and all of them once it has finished; llama-server is started with `--threads` set to the other half, and `OMP_NUM_THREADS` is left unset (README).
- Startup also warms the LLM: it reads the system prompt once, so the first reply's words come about 2 s sooner (0.6 s instead of 2.5 s to the first token).
- The browser asks for clips with the count it has, and the server answers the moment there is a new one (or after 10 s), instead of every 0.3 s; the next clip loads in a second player while the current one plays, so the switch between sentences costs nothing.
- Under the video, the chat page shows how long each sentence took from its text arriving to being spoken, and the pause after the sentence before. The first sentence is in red when its wait passes 2 s, the others when their pause does. The log has `Engine threads set`, `LLM warmed up` and `since_sentence_ms` on `Sentence clip ready`.
- Measured with this PR on the 4-core CPU (Gemma 3 4B, Kokoro, photoreal), for a reply of three sentences of about 5.5 s of speech each: the first sentence was spoken 8.2 s after its text arrived (Kokoro 3.8 s and the video 4.3 s, each on 2 threads while the LLM wrote the rest), and the next two followed it with no pause. The first clip's wait is now the whole problem.
- **Can show:** the timing readout under the video, with the measured gaps.
- **Built:** as above. The first clip's wait comes from speaking and then rendering the whole first sentence after its text arrives, on half the cores; R22b cuts the first sentence at a clause so the first clip is short. With the person's own voice (Chatterbox, about 2.3 s of work per second of speech on 4 cores) the target is out of reach on the CPU alone; see the PR.

### R22b. Quick clips: the first clause first (Medium) · item 12 · Done in [PR #50](https://github.com/larry94555/ImageSkinForLLM/pull/50)
- The reply's first sentence is handed to the clips at its first clause, so the first clip is short and is spoken while the LLM is still writing the rest of the sentence: at a comma, semicolon, colon or dash after at least 3 words, before a joining word such as "and" or "because", or after 8 to 12 words when there is none (not on a word that leads into the next). The clause counts as a sentence in the log and the readout, whose rows are now "Clip N".
- Each clip's two steps, the voice and the video, are apart in the code (`ClipSteps`, `Sentence spoken` with `speak_ms` in the log), so the next PRs can play the video while it is still being made. They run one after the other: measured on the 4-core CPU, speaking a sentence while the one before it rendered made Kokoro about twice as slow (real-time factor 0.5 to 1.1) and the video too, with nothing gained.
- Measured with this PR (Gemma 3 4B in llama-server with 2 threads, Kokoro, photoreal, on the 4-core CPU): the first clause's text arrived 2.8 s after Send instead of 4.6 s for the whole sentence, and its clip was spoken 4.2 s after its text for a 6-word clause (Kokoro 1.1 s, the video 3.1 s) and 9.2 s for an 8-word one on a slower run (Kokoro 3.5 s, the video 5.5 s). Those video times were the proof harness's, not the product's: the harness wrote WebM with libvpx, whose default of one thread per core took 80 ms a frame beside the LLM against 4 ms with two threads, while the product's x264 encoder and its compositing run at the same speed beside the LLM as alone (measured for R22c). Kokoro runs at a real-time factor of 0.5 to 1.0 beside the LLM against 0.45 alone. So the next steps are R22c, cheaper frames (each frame was pasted and blended in floating point over the whole face, 10 to 13 ms a frame), and R22d, playing each clip while it is still being written, so the video step leaves the wait and the voice step is most of what is left.
- **Can show:** the first clip playing while the LLM is still writing the first sentence, and the readout.
- **Built:** as above. The video step's slowness beside the LLM measured here turned out to be the proof harness's WebM encoder, not the product's (see R22c).

### R22c. Quick clips: cheaper frames (Medium) · item 12 · Done in [PR #51](https://github.com/larry94555/ImageSkinForLLM/pull/51)
- Each frame of a clip is composited by OpenCV in 8-bit where it was blended in floating point over the whole face: the mouth and eyes are blended into the face with `cv2.blendLinear`, the head's movement is one `cv2.remap` whose maps are made by `cv2.addWeighted` and `cv2.scaleAdd` from grids made once, the face is pasted into the photo the same way, and the stills around the mouth and eyes are kept rather than copied for each frame. Measured on the 4-core CPU: about 4 ms a frame instead of 10 to 13, so the video step's real-time factor once the LLM has finished is 0.17 to 0.2 instead of 0.4 to 0.5; the frames differ from before by at most 2 of 255 in any pixel (rounding).
- Reply clips are rendered on the photo scaled down to 720 pixels on its longest side (`REPLY_SIDE` in `prepare_job.py`): the chat page shows them at most 640 pixels wide, and a frame of a 1280-pixel photo took 34 ms to paste and encode against 8 ms at 720 and 3.5 ms at 512. The sample video keeps the photo's size.
- Found on the way: the video step's slowness beside the LLM measured for R22b (real-time factor 1.4 to 1.6) was the proof harness's WebM encoder (libvpx) with its default of one thread per core, which took 80 ms a frame beside the LLM against 4 ms with two threads; the product's x264 encoder takes 4 ms a frame either way, and the compositing is not slowed by the LLM at all (6 ms a frame both ways, measured apart from the app). The harness now gives libvpx two threads. So the voice is the wait that is left: measured with this PR (same setup as R22b), the first clip was spoken 2.4 s after its text for a 6-word clause (Kokoro 1.6 s, the video 0.8 s) and 4.3 s for an 8-word one (Kokoro 3.3 s, the video 1.1 s); beside the LLM Kokoro runs at a real-time factor of 0.5 to 0.9 against 0.45 alone. Over the API with the product's own MP4 encoder (no browser), the first clip was ready 1.7 s after its text for the 6-word clause (Kokoro 1.1 s, the video 0.6 s) and 3.0 s for the 8-word one (Kokoro 2.0 s, the video 1.0 s). Next: R22d plays each clip while it is still being written, so the video leaves the wait; then R22e cuts the first clause shorter; the voice step itself is the floor (see the note on a faster voice for a 4-core CPU in the project files).
- **Can show:** the first clip about 2.5 s after its text for a short clause, with the readout saying so.
- **Built:** as above.

### R22d. Quick clips: play each clip while it is written (Medium) · item 12 · Done in [PR #52](https://github.com/larry94555/ImageSkinForLLM/pull/52)
- A reply clip is listed for the browser the moment its video step starts, not once its video is whole: the clip is written as a fragmented MP4, half a second of video at a time with no look-ahead in the encoder (`progressive` in `video.write_mp4`), and `GET /api/chat/clips/{id}/{n}` streams it as it grows (`SentenceClips.follow`), whole as a file once it is rendered. A plain `<video>` plays a clip served that way while the rest is written, so the video step leaves the wait before a clip; `SentenceClips.rendered` counts the whole clips, and a clip whose video fails is unlisted and its stream ended. The timing readout's "spoken after its text" now measures from the text to the clip starting to play; the log's `Sentence clip ready` has `listed_since_sentence_ms` (text to the clip listed, as its video starts to be written; it plays once its first fragment is out) next to `since_sentence_ms` (text to whole).
- Measured with this PR (same setup as R22c, the two prompts of R22b): over the API with the product's MP4 encoder, the first clip was listed for the browser (its video starting to be written) 1.8 s after its text for the 8-word clause and 1.1 s for the 6-word one, against 2.6 s and 1.9 s to whole; the later clips 1.1 to 3.6 s after their text, all of it the voice step. A clip plays once its first half-second fragment is written, a little after it is listed; the readout in the browser measures that. In Chromium on the same 4 cores (the browser's decoding takes cores from the engines, and it buffers about half a second of a live stream before it plays), the first clip was spoken 3.9 s and 2.6 s after its text (R22c: 4.5 s and 2.4 s). Chromium starts a live stream the same 0.7 to 0.9 s in whether its fragments are 100, 250 or 500 ms long, so the fragment length stays at half a second.
- Found on the way: the voice step is now the whole wait before a clip, and beside the LLM it runs at a real-time factor of 0.5 to 0.9 (Kokoro, 2 threads), so clips cannot keep up with playing while the LLM writes: the pauses between later clips are the engines' throughput, not a wait that code can remove. What is left to reach 1 to 2 s for the first clip: a shorter first clause (R22e), and a faster voice (see the note on a faster voice for a 4-core CPU in the project files) or a graphics card for the rest.
- **Can show:** the clip starts playing before its video is finished; the readout counts from the text to the clip playing.
- **Built:** as above.

### R22e. Quick clips: a shorter first clause (Simple) · item 12 · Done in [PR #53](https://github.com/larry94555/ImageSkinForLLM/pull/53)
- The first clause is cut after 5 words instead of 8 when the first sentence has no clause boundary (`FIRST_CLAUSE_MAX` in `sentences.py`; the stretch for a word that leads into the next is now up to 8 words, `FIRST_CLAUSE_LIMIT`, instead of 12). When the fifth word leads into the next ("Martin Luther King Jr. was"), the clause ends on the last word before it that doesn't ("Martin Luther King Jr."), rather than running on. A cut by word count waits for the word after the next, so a sentence that ends on the next word ("The capital of France is Paris.") stays whole and no clip is left with one word.
- Measured apart from the app (Kokoro, 2 threads, idle): the voice step grows with the clause, 1.5 s for the 4-word "Martin Luther King Junior." (2.2 s of speech) against 2.2 s for the 8-word clause (3.55 s), so the first clip is ready about 0.7 s sooner alone and more beside the LLM. The cost: the rest of the sentence is a longer clip whose text arrives when the sentence ends, so the pause before clip 2 can grow by about as much; the engines' throughput beside the LLM sets the total.
- **Can show:** the first clip is a few words and starts sooner; the readout says so.
- **Built:** as above.

### R22f. Quick clips: a fast clone of the person's voice (Medium) · items 4, 12 · Done in [PR #55](https://github.com/larry94555/ImageSkinForLLM/pull/55)
- Added after Larry's test of R22e (2026-10-11): replies in his cloned voice still waited well over 2 seconds. Measured on 4 cores with 2 threads, Chatterbox Turbo took 7 s for a 4-word clause and 8.8 s for a 6-word one (about four times slower than real time), while Pocket TTS (Kyutai; code MIT, weights CC-BY-4.0), which also clones from 10 seconds of the voice sample and keeps the person's accent, took 0.56 s and 0.77 s (int8). Kokoro, not the person's voice, took 1.3 s.
- With Pocket TTS installed next to Chatterbox, it speaks everything in the person's own accent (`pocket_engine.py`, run through `ChatterboxEngine`'s steps with the same forced alignment), so the replies, the sample and the fixed lines sound the same; with another accent, Kokoro's base voice reads a passage once in Prepare, Chatterbox's converter turns it into the person's voice and Pocket learns from that, so it speaks every line with that accent at the same speed (`AccentEngine` with `clone_keeps_accent`). Without Pocket, another accent converts each line, about 17 to 22 s a clip on Larry's laptop. Installing or removing it, then restarting the server (the engine is picked at startup), asks for Prepare again. Its cloning weights are gated: the README says how to accept the terms and sign in once.
- Found on the way: Pocket generates and decodes on two threads of its own, which start with PyTorch's thread count, so beside the LLM they used twice the engines' share of the cores and a 4-word clause took 1.5 s; splitting the engines' threads between them brought it to about 0.75 s.
- Measured in the chat page (Gemma 3 4B in llama.cpp with 2 threads, photoreal, Chromium on the same 4-core CPU, Pocket's preset voice standing in for a clone since the cloning weights need a sign-in): the first clip started 1.5 and 1.6 s after its text, and the average wait before a clip was 0.8 s over 6 clips.
- **Can show:** the timing readout's average under 2 s in the person's own voice.

### R22g. Quick clips: the performance cores (Simple) · item 12 · Done in [PR #56](https://github.com/larry94555/ImageSkinForLLM/pull/56)
- Added after Larry's test of R22f on his laptop (2026-10-11), an Intel i7-1360P with 4 performance cores (2 threads each) and 8 efficiency cores. The app counted 16 cores and spread its threads over all of them, though an efficiency core is about 3 times slower per thread. Kept on the performance cores with 8 threads, Pocket TTS took 0.38 to 0.45 seconds of work per second of speech against 0.6 to 1.2, and a 6-word clip was ready in about 1.8 s against 2 to 4.5 s (measured on his laptop with a timing script).
- On a CPU with performance and efficiency cores, the app keeps itself on the performance cores at startup and counts only theirs for sharing the cores with the LLM (`use_performance_cores` in `cores.py`): on Windows from the cores' efficiency classes, on Linux from Intel's list of performance cores. `IMAGESKIN_ALL_CORES=1` turns it off. Other CPUs, and macOS, are unchanged.
- **Can show:** the log's `Kept the app on the performance cores` line, and shorter waits in the timing readout on such a laptop.

### R23. Idle video and smooth joins (Medium) · item 17 · Done in [PR #61](https://github.com/larry94555/ImageSkinForLLM/pull/61)
- Between replies the person blinks and moves slightly instead of freezing.
- Returns to the idle pose between sentence clips and crossfades the joins.
- **Can show:** the person looks alive while waiting, and multi-sentence replies play without visible jumps.
- **Built:** the warm-up makes an idle video once per photo (`render_idle` in `photoreal.py`, 24 s: three turns of the idle loop with the mouth at rest and blinks, silent, at the reply clips' size; about 3 s to make), kept in `<data folder>/replies/idle` and served at `GET /api/chat/idle`. The chat page plays it on a loop under the reply clips (`Stage` in `pages.tsx`): a clip fades in over it as it starts (0.25 s), and the player fades back to it when it runs out of clips, during a pause between clips, after the reply and after Stop. Each clip starts on the idle-loop frame the one before it ended on, and the voice's head and brow movement eases out over the first and last 5 frames of a clip (`settled`), so back-to-back clips join without a jump: measured on the real engines, the mean pixel change at a join went from 2.45 and 1.53 to 0.23 and 0.14, against 0.2 between two frames inside a clip. Without an idle video (an engine that makes none, or it failed), the chat holds each reply's last frame as before. The log has `Rendered idle video` and `Idle video ready` with the time taken, or `Could not make the idle video`; the browser console has `Idle video ready`.

## Milestone 6: Content and the wiki

| PR | Description | Status |
|---|---|---|
| [R34. PDF storage and content API](#r34-pdf-storage-and-content-api-medium--item-24--done-in-pr-62) | A server API to add, remove and replace PDFs, stored safely. | Done in [PR #62](https://github.com/larry94555/ImageSkinForLLM/pull/62) |
| [R35. Manage Content page](#r35-manage-content-page-medium--item-24--done-in-pr-63) | A Manage Content page to add, remove and replace PDFs. | Done in [PR #63](https://github.com/larry94555/ImageSkinForLLM/pull/63) |
| [R36. Reading text, slides and tables](#r36-reading-text-slides-and-tables-medium--item-25--done-in-pr-64) | Reads the text, slides and tables out of each PDF, keeping page numbers. | Done in [PR #64](https://github.com/larry94555/ImageSkinForLLM/pull/64) |
| [R37. Building the wiki](#r37-building-the-wiki-medium--item-26--done-in-pr-67) | The LLM builds wiki pages from the PDFs, linking back to their pages. | Done in [PR #67](https://github.com/larry94555/ImageSkinForLLM/pull/67) |
| [R37a. The wiki as an outline](#r37a-the-wiki-as-an-outline-medium--item-26--done-in-pr-68) | Builds the wiki as an outline of topics and detail pages. | Done in [PR #68](https://github.com/larry94555/ImageSkinForLLM/pull/68) |
| [R37b. Reading the wiki in the browser](#r37b-reading-the-wiki-in-the-browser-medium--item-26--done-in-pr-69) | Readable wiki pages in the browser, with an outline that opens and closes. | Done in [PR #69](https://github.com/larry94555/ImageSkinForLLM/pull/69) |
| [R38. Editing the wiki](#r38-editing-the-wiki-medium--item-26--done-in-pr-70) | Edit and save wiki entries, with edits kept when the wiki is rebuilt. | Done in [PR #70](https://github.com/larry94555/ImageSkinForLLM/pull/70) |
| [R38a. Wiki versions and saving it to a file](#r38a-wiki-versions-and-saving-it-to-a-file-medium--item-26--done-in-pr-71) | Wiki versions to go back to, and saving the wiki to a file and loading it. | Done in [PR #71](https://github.com/larry94555/ImageSkinForLLM/pull/71) |

### R34. PDF storage and content API (Medium) · item 24 · Done in [PR #62](https://github.com/larry94555/ImageSkinForLLM/pull/62)
- Server API to add, remove and replace PDFs, stored apart from the image and sound files, with the same safe-storage rules as R6 (size limit, type check, safe names).
- **Can show:** add, replace and remove a PDF with API calls, and the logs and folder showing each change.
- **Built:** `ContentStore` in `content.py` keeps PDFs in `<data folder>/content/pdfs` as `<id>.pdf` with `<id>.json` (display name, size, `added_at`, `replaced_at`); `POST`, `GET`, `PUT` and `DELETE` under `/api/content`. The type is checked from the first bytes (`%PDF-`), names are only for display, and limits are 100 MB per PDF and 2 GB in total. Replacing keeps the id, so R37 can rebuild only that PDF's wiki pages. The API needs no consent (PDFs aren't the person's likeness); R44 adds the administrator sign-on.

### R35. Manage Content page (Medium) · item 24 · Done in [PR #63](https://github.com/larry94555/ImageSkinForLLM/pull/63)
- A "Manage Content" link opens a separate page listing the PDFs, with add, remove and replace.
- **Can show:** in the browser, upload two PDFs, replace one and remove the other.
- **Built:** `ContentPage` in `web/src/content.tsx` at `#/content`, linked as **Manage Content** in the top bar. It lists each PDF with its name (opens the PDF), size and the day it was added or last replaced; **Add PDFs** takes several at a time, **Replace** keeps the PDF's place and id, **Remove** asks first, and a refused file is shown with the server's reason. The browser console has `Added …` and `Replaced … with …` with the time taken in ms, and `Removed …`. The top bar now wraps on a narrow screen.

### R36. Reading text, slides and tables (Medium) · item 25 · Done in [PR #64](https://github.com/larry94555/ImageSkinForLLM/pull/64)
- Extracts each PDF by kind: text as paragraphs, slides page by page, tables as rows and columns, with the page number kept for each piece. Runs when a PDF is added or replaced, as a job with progress like R12.
- **Can show:** one PDF of each kind, and the extracted text and tables it produced.
- **Built:** `read_pdf` in `pdf_reading.py` reads a PDF with pdfplumber: tables from their ruling lines as rows, a page wider than tall as one slide, other text as paragraphs split at the gaps between lines, each piece with its page. `ReadingJob` in `reading_job.py` reads one PDF at a time in the background when it is added or replaced, and on start any not read yet, and saves `content/read/<id>.json` with the PDF's version so an old reading is never used. `GET /api/content/readings` gives the progress and `GET /api/content/{id}/reading` what was read; Manage Content shows "Reading page N of M…", then the counts found, with a **What was read** link. Pages with no text (scans) are listed, not read: no OCR yet.

### R37. Building the wiki (Medium) · item 26 · Done in [PR #67](https://github.com/larry94555/ImageSkinForLLM/pull/67)
- The LLM turns the extracted content into wiki pages (one per topic, linking back to the PDF pages it came from). Adding, replacing or removing a PDF rebuilds only the pages that PDF affects.
- **Can show:** the wiki pages built from a sample PDF, and the logs showing how long the build took.
- **Built:** `build_wiki` in `wiki.py` cuts a PDF's reading into parts that fit the context window (each piece marked `[page N]`), asks the LLM for each part's topics as JSON (title, pages, text; at most 6, held to a schema on llama-server), and merges a topic that runs over several parts, since the LLM is told the titles found so far. An unusable answer is asked again once, then the part is skipped and listed. `WikiJob` in `wiki_job.py` builds one PDF at a time after it is read, saves `content/wiki/<id>.json` with the PDF's version, rebuilds only a replaced PDF's pages, removes a removed PDF's, catches up on start, and waits for llama-server when it can't be reached. Each PDF has its own pages, so the same topic in two PDFs is two pages *(default)*. `GET /api/wiki`, `/api/wiki/{page_id}`, `/api/wiki/builds` and `/api/content/{id}/wiki`; Manage Content shows the progress, then the page count with a **Wiki pages** link.

### R37a. The wiki as an outline (Medium) · item 26 · Done in [PR #68](https://github.com/larry94555/ImageSkinForLLM/pull/68)
- Added by Larry on 2026-10-12: the wiki must read as an outline, not a flat list. From the topics R37 finds, the LLM builds each PDF's table of contents as an outline of sections. Each entry is either summarized in a few sentences, when its detail is simple enough, or has a short summary with a link to a detail page of its own. A detail page follows the same pattern: a few sentences on the whole, then an outline of its parts, each summarized or linked to a further detail page.
- Simple enough *(default)*: its text fits in about 5 sentences. Depth is capped at 4 levels, so a long PDF can't make the build run on.
- **Can show:** the outline and detail pages built from a sample PDF (as JSON for now), and the logs showing each level's time.
- **Built:** `wiki_outline.py` builds the outline in the same job, after the topics. With more than 3 topics the LLM groups them into sections (a topic it leaves out stays on its own). Each topic keeps the LLM's few sentences as its summary. When the PDF says more on the topic's pages than 5 sentences (or 600 characters), the topic gets a detail page: the LLM groups the PDF's paragraphs about it into parts, and each part is again shown in the PDF's own words or opened, down to 4 levels. This changed after Larry's test (2026-10-12), where no entry had a detail page: the first version opened a topic only when the LLM's own write-up of it ran past 5 sentences, which a good model's rarely does. Whatever the LLM can't group or split is kept as it is, and a refused outline keeps the topics as they are. Larry found the build slow (2026-10-12): most of each request's time was the LLM writing its answer, so the LLM now writes only part titles and paragraph numbers instead of the PDF's text again, and topic write-ups are 2 or 3 sentences instead of 3 to 8; Manage Content shows the time the build has taken so far, about how long its stage has left (Larry, 2026-10-12), and how long it took. The outline is saved with the pages (pages saved by R37 are built again on start), `GET /api/wiki/{page_id}` serves any entry with its parts down to the next detail pages, and Manage Content shows "Building the wiki outline: step N of about M…" then "Wiki: N pages, M detail pages". With Qwen 2.5 1.5B on 4 CPU cores, the 4-page setup guide's topics took 11 outline requests (233 s) and gave 1 section and 5 detail pages. The small model's parts overlap where topics share a page and some are titled just "Part 1", so a bigger model should give a better outline.

### R37b. Reading the wiki in the browser (Medium) · item 26 · Done in [PR #69](https://github.com/larry94555/ImageSkinForLLM/pull/69)
- Replaces the JSON link with readable pages: a **Wiki** page lists every PDF's table of contents as a nested outline that opens and closes. Each entry shows its summary; an entry with a detail page links to it, and that page opens on its own address with the same layout, a trail back to the levels above it, and links to the PDF pages it came from.
- **Can show:** open a PDF's table of contents, open and close its levels, follow a link to a detail page and back, and open a cited PDF page.
- **Built:** `wiki.tsx` adds a **Wiki** page to the top bar (`#/wiki`, every PDF; `#/wiki/<PDF id>`, one PDF, which Manage Content's **Read the wiki** link opens in place of the JSON). The table of contents shows every level of the outline *(default)*; each entry with parts opens and closes on a click, with **Open all** and **Close all**, and each entry shows its summary and its PDF pages, each opening that page of the PDF in a new tab. A detail entry's title and **Read more** link open its page (`#/wiki/page/<entry id>`): a trail back to the PDF and the detail pages above it, its summary, PDF pages and parts, down to the next detail pages. A PDF whose wiki isn't built yet says so and points to Manage Content. `GET /api/wiki/contents` serves the tables of contents, and `GET /api/wiki/{page_id}` now has the `trail`. Shown with the setup guide and a 6-page lesson PDF built by Qwen 2.5 1.5B; the small model gave the lesson only 2 topics and no detail pages, and some parts are long runs of the PDF's own words, which a bigger model should improve.

### R38. Editing the wiki (Medium) · item 26 · Done in [PR #70](https://github.com/larry94555/ImageSkinForLLM/pull/70)
- Larry asked on 2026-10-13 that the user can edit the wiki pages, save them, load them and regenerate them. Browsing the pages and the PDF pages each came from is R37b's Wiki page.
- **Edit and save:** on the Wiki page and its detail pages, an **Edit** button turns an entry's title and summary into text boxes, with **Save** and **Cancel**. A saved entry is marked as edited.
- **Load:** edits are saved on the server beside the built wiki, so they are there whenever the Wiki page is opened again, after a restart too.
- **Regenerate:** a **Rebuild the wiki** button for each PDF builds it again with the LLM. Edited entries are kept *(default)*: an edited entry whose topic the rebuild finds again keeps the user's text and is flagged for review, and one it no longer finds is listed so nothing is lost silently. Replacing the PDF works the same way.
- **Can show:** edit a summary and save it, reload the page and see it kept, rebuild the wiki and see the edit kept and flagged, and cancel an edit.
- **Built:** `wiki_edits.py` keeps each PDF's edits in `content/wiki/<id>.edits.json`, apart from the built pages, and `WikiJob` shows them over what was built, marks them all for review whenever a new build is saved, lists the ones whose entries are gone, and removes them with the PDF. The Wiki page and detail pages have **Edit** (title and summary, **Save**, **Cancel**), "Edited by you" with **Undo my edit**, and, after a rebuild, what the new wiki says with **Keep my edit** or **Use the new text**. **Rebuild the wiki** asks once more, shows the progress and loads the new pages when done; the old pages stay readable meanwhile. Entries are matched by id, which comes from the title, so an entry the rebuild titles differently counts as gone and its edit is listed *(default)*. Shown with Qwen 2.5 1.5B on the 6-page lesson PDF: an edit, a reload, a 44 s rebuild that kept and flagged the edit, and **Keep my edit**.

### R38a. Wiki versions and saving it to a file (Medium) · item 26 · Done in [PR #71](https://github.com/larry94555/ImageSkinForLLM/pull/71)
- Larry asked on 2026-10-13 that the wiki can be saved to a file and loaded back if it is rebuilt by accident, that edits made before a rebuild are kept, and that there is a version history to return the wiki to an earlier state. Only the current state is in use, but the history of changes is kept with it. R38 already keeps edits across rebuilds; this step adds the history and the file.
- **History:** every change to a PDF's wiki saves a new version of the whole wiki as it then reads (the built pages with the edits), with when it was made and what changed: built or rebuilt by the LLM, an entry edited, an edit undone or kept, a lost edit discarded, or an earlier version restored. Versions are kept on the server in `content/wiki/<id>.history/`, all of them *(default)*, so an accidental rebuild loses nothing.
- **Going back:** a **History** button for each PDF on the Wiki page lists its versions, newest first. Any version can be opened to read, then **Restore**d: it becomes the current wiki as a new version, so a restore can itself be undone.
- **Saving to a file:** **Save to a file** downloads the PDF's current wiki with its whole history as one file. **Load from a file** reads one back: its current state becomes the wiki and its history is kept, after checking the file is for the same PDF (if the PDF has been replaced since, it says so and asks first).
- **Can show:** edit an entry, rebuild, and return to the version before the rebuild; save the wiki to a file, rebuild it, load the file and see the wiki and its history back.

## Milestone 7: Answers from the content

| PR | Description | Status |
|---|---|---|
| [R39. Looking up questions in the wiki and PDFs](#r39-looking-up-questions-in-the-wiki-and-pdfs-medium--item-27--done-in-pr-72) | Finds the wiki pages and PDF passages that match a question. | Done in [PR #72](https://github.com/larry94555/ImageSkinForLLM/pull/72) |
| [R40. Answering from the content](#r40-answering-from-the-content-medium--item-27--done-in-pr-73) | The LLM answers questions from the passages found in the content. | Done in [PR #73](https://github.com/larry94555/ImageSkinForLLM/pull/73) |
| [R41. Question scope setting](#r41-question-scope-setting-simple--item-28--done-in-pr-74) | A setting for whether questions outside the content are answered. | Done in [PR #74](https://github.com/larry94555/ImageSkinForLLM/pull/74) |

### R39. Looking up questions in the wiki and PDFs (Medium) · item 27 · Done in [PR #72](https://github.com/larry94555/ImageSkinForLLM/pull/72)
- Finds the wiki pages and PDF passages that match a question, using the method decided before this PR.
- **Can show:** a command that prints the passages found for a few sample questions, with the time the lookup took.
- **Built:** `lookup.py` ranks every wiki entry (title and summary, with the user's edits, every level of the outline) and every paragraph, slide and table read from the PDFs with BM25, ranked together so a word's rarity is judged across all the content; words are matched in lower case, without common words and with a plural "s" or an "ing" or "ed" ending left off, and a wiki entry's title counts twice. A passage is found only when it has at least 40% of the question's words, weighted by how rare each is in the content (a word the content lacks weighs the most). This was added after Larry's test (2026-10-13), where, with only a paper on primes added, "What lighting is best for the photo?" found the paper's thanks because it says "best"; it now finds nothing. The passages are gathered again for each question, so an edit or rebuild is found at once. `imageskin lookup "question" …` prints the best 3 wiki entries and 5 PDF passages for each question, with their scores, pages and the time taken; the log has `Looked up a question`.

### R40. Answering from the content (Medium) · item 27 · Done in [PR #73](https://github.com/larry94555/ImageSkinForLLM/pull/73)
- The chat sends the passages found by R39 with the question, and the LLM answers from them; the answer is shown and spoken as in Milestones 4 and 5.
- **Can show:** ask a question about a sample PDF and hear the person answer it correctly.
- **Built:** `Conversation` takes a `find` that `create_app` wires to the R39 lookup: each prompt's best wiki entries and PDF passages (best first, each with its PDF and pages, cut to 400 characters) are quoted before the prompt in its own message, between `<<<` and `>>>` as reference text that is source material only, never instructions to follow (review on PR #73), with the LLM asked to answer from them in its own words without mentioning documents or pages. Only the prompt is kept in the conversation and the system prompt is unchanged, so llama-server keeps reusing what it read. The passages take at most 300 tokens, the last one cut at a word to fit: the LLM reads them all before its first word, and with Qwen 2.5 1.5B on 4 CPU cores 800 tokens of them held the first word back 7 s; with 300 it starts in about 3 s. A prompt nothing matches is sent alone, and general prompts were still answered (R41 adds the setting). Shown with the two setup guides and the Riemann primes paper: "How long should each voice recording be?" went from "It depends on what you want to convey" to "about 1 to 2 minutes long", and the lighting and install questions were answered from the guides. The log has `Content sent with the prompt` with `passages_found` and `passages_sent`.

### R41. Question scope setting (Simple) · item 28 · Done in [PR #74](https://github.com/larry94555/ImageSkinForLLM/pull/74)
- A setting for whether general prompts are allowed. When they aren't, an off-topic prompt gets a short, polite message instead of an answer. The Settings page comes later (R27), so for now the setting is in the config file.
- **Can show:** the same off-topic question answered with the setting on and politely declined with it off.
- **Built:** `general_prompts` in the config file, false by default as item 28 says. When it is false and the R39 lookup finds nothing for a prompt, the chat replies "Sorry, I can only answer questions about the material I was given. Try asking about something in it." without asking the LLM, spoken like any reply; the decline is shown in the chat but not sent to the LLM with later prompts. Before any PDF is added every prompt is answered, as there is nothing to keep to, and a prompt whose lookup fails is answered rather than declined. A follow-up like "tell me more" is declined too, since it has none of the content's words. Larry's test with the Riemann paper (2026-10-13): "How is the zeta function used by Reimann?" was declined, as the paper writes ζ, says "use", and spells Riemann right; the lookup now matches Greek letters by name, leaves a final "e" off ("used" matches "use"), and takes a question word of 5 or more letters that the content lacks as the content word one typo away in a passage that also has another of its words as typed (so "What is polar?" isn't taken as "solar", review on PR #74), and it is answered. The log has `Question scope` at start-up and `Prompt declined: general prompts are off`.

| [R41a. Settings menu: show metrics](#r41a-settings-menu-show-metrics-medium--items-49-51--done-in-pr-76) | A Settings menu whose first setting shows or hides the timing metrics. | Done in [PR #76](https://github.com/larry94555/ImageSkinForLLM/pull/76) |
| [R41b. Participant view and host view](#r41b-participant-view-and-host-view-medium--items-50-51--done-in-pr-77) | A setting to switch between the host view and the simpler participant view. | Done in [PR #77](https://github.com/larry94555/ImageSkinForLLM/pull/77) |
| [R41c. Clean video playback](#r41c-clean-video-playback-medium--items-11-17-48--done-in-pr-78) | The video looks like one steady picture, with no flashes, jumps or empty frames. | Done in [PR #78](https://github.com/larry94555/ImageSkinForLLM/pull/78) |
| [R41d. A professional chat screen](#r41d-a-professional-chat-screen-medium--items-9-11-20-48--done-in-pr-80) | One consistent, calm look for the chat screen. | Done in [PR #80](https://github.com/larry94555/ImageSkinForLLM/pull/80) |
| [R41e. Professional host pages](#r41e-professional-host-pages-medium--items-24-26-48-50--done-in-pr-82) | The same look and plain wording on the Setup, Manage Content and Wiki pages. | Done in [PR #82](https://github.com/larry94555/ImageSkinForLLM/pull/82) |
| [R41e2. A designed look](#r41e2-a-designed-look-medium--items-48-50--done-in-pr-83) | One professional design across every page, including phones and dark mode. | Done in [PR #83](https://github.com/larry94555/ImageSkinForLLM/pull/83) |

### R41a. Settings menu: show metrics (Medium) · items 49, 51 · Done in [PR #76](https://github.com/larry94555/ImageSkinForLLM/pull/76)
- A **Settings** menu in the top bar of every page, open to anyone for now. Its first setting, **Show timing metrics**, is off by default: the timing readout under the video (R22a) and the other timings and counts shown on the pages (such as how long a step or a search took) appear only when it is on. The log keeps every timing either way.
- The settings are saved on the server, so they stay the same after a reload or restart and on every browser *(default)*.
- **Can show:** the chat with no timing readout, then with it after turning the setting on, and still on after a reload.
- **Built:** a **Settings** link at the right of the top bar opens a small menu with **Show timing metrics**, off by default. Off, the chat has no timing readout under the video, a finished setup step says "Done" rather than "Done in 31 s", and a wiki build says how long is left but not how long it has taken ("Wiki: 6 pages" rather than "Wiki: 6 pages, built in 18 min 20 s"). How long is left stays, since it tells a host how long to wait. The settings are saved as `ui-settings.json` in the app's data folder through `GET` and `PATCH /api/settings`, which need no consent; an unknown setting or a value that isn't true or false is refused, and an unreadable file gives the defaults. The browser console keeps logging every timing either way, and the server logs `Settings changed`.

### R41b. Participant view and host view (Medium) · items 50, 51 · Done in [PR #77](https://github.com/larry94555/ImageSkinForLLM/pull/77)
- A second setting in the Settings menu: **View: Host or Participant**, Host by default so nothing changes until it is set *(default)*.
- **Participant view:** the top bar has only the chat and the wiki. Setup and Manage Content are hidden, and so are every way to change the wiki: Edit, Undo my edit, Keep my edit or Use the new text, Rebuild the wiki, versions, and saving or loading it from a file. The wiki reads as in R37b, and each PDF can be opened read-only from the wiki. Opening a hidden page by its address shows the chat instead.
- **Host view:** every page and option, as today.
- Only the screens change for now: the server doesn't check the view, since anyone can switch it. R44's sign-on makes the host's pages the administrator's.
- **Can show:** switch to Participant and see only the chat and a read-only wiki with its PDFs, switch back to Host and see every option again.
- **Built:** the Settings menu has **View: Host** (every page and option) or **Participant** (the chat and a read-only wiki), saved with the other settings as `view`, Host by default. In the participant view the top bar has only Chat and Wiki, and the name at its left opens the chat. Home, Setup, the recording guide, Manage Content and the consent page, opened by their address, show the chat instead. The wiki has no Rebuild, History, Save to a file, Load from a file, Edit, Undo my edit or Keep my edit buttons, no edits left by a rebuild, no "Edited by you", and no links to Manage Content. Every PDF in the wiki now has an **Open the PDF** link (in both views), and its page numbers open it at that page as before. A chat whose setup isn't finished tells a participant "Ask your host to finish setting it up" rather than linking to setup. The app waits for the settings before it shows a page, so a host's page doesn't flash up first. On 2026-10-19 the views were renamed from Teacher and Student: the Settings page shows **Host (Teacher)** and **Participant (Student)**, saved as `host` and `participant`, and a saved `teacher` or `student` is read as the new name. Later the words in parentheses follow the primary context (R84 on).

### R41c. Clean video playback (Medium) · items 11, 17, 48 · Done in [PR #78](https://github.com/larry94555/ImageSkinForLLM/pull/78)
- The video looks like one steady picture of the person from the moment the chat opens: no browser video controls, no black or empty frames while a clip or the idle video loads (the photo shows until the idle video plays), no jumps in size or position, and no visible flicker or stall when a reply starts, between sentences, at the end and after Stop.
- Anything that goes wrong with a clip is handled quietly: the reply text still shows, as in item 20, with no broken player left on the screen.
- **Can show:** a recording of the chat from opening it through two replies and a Stop, with no flash, jump or empty frame, compared with the same steps before this PR.
- **Built:** the person's place on the chat page keeps one size from the moment the page opens (45% of the window's height), and the photo the videos are made from lies under the videos, the same size and in the same place, with the page's own colour around it rather than black. So the photo shows while the idle video loads, the idle video fades in over it once it plays, and each reply's first clip fades in once it plays rather than showing an empty player. The person shows while the conversation loads. Stop pauses the clip at once and fades back to the idle video, or holds its frame when there is none, rather than cutting. A clip that can't be played is passed over: the browser console logs which, the clips after it still play, and nothing broken is left on the screen. The **Play the reply** button sits over the person. In a browser run of the chat against a stand-in server, from opening it through two replies and a Stop, the stage showed nothing of the person in 152 of 806 frames before this change and changed size once (150 to 342 pixels high, when the idle video came); after it, 2 of 980 frames (the first 17 ms, before the photo arrives) and no change of size.

### R41d. A professional chat screen (Medium) · items 9, 11, 20, 48 · Done in [PR #80](https://github.com/larry94555/ImageSkinForLLM/pull/80)
- The chat screen gets one consistent, calm look: the person's video as the centre of the page, a clear prompt box and Send button, a tidy reply text panel, and a simple "thinking" state while the answer is on its way.
- Every message a participant can see is short, plain and friendly, with no error codes, file names, ids or technical words; technical detail goes to the log.
- It works on a laptop screen and a tablet, and is easy to read (sizes, contrast, keyboard use).
- **Can show:** screenshots of the chat on a laptop and a tablet: before a question, while thinking, after an answer, with the text panel open, and when the LLM is down.
- **Built:** on a screen at least 900 pixels wide (a laptop, or a tablet held sideways), the person is on the left in a frame the shape of their photo, so there are no empty bands around them, that stays in view (the server says the photo's size through `GET /api/chat/photo`, and the frame is drawn only once it is known, so it never changes size), with Stop, Mute and Volume under them, and the conversation is on the right; on a narrower screen (a tablet held upright) the person is above the conversation. The conversation shows the latest question and reply as rounded cards, one status line ("Thinking…", then "Getting ready to say it…" until the first clip or the reply's video actually starts playing), and the question box with **Send** beside it. The page title is kept for screen readers only, so the person starts at the top. When there is no reply, the chat says "Sorry, I couldn't answer that just now. Please try again in a moment." whatever the reason (the LLM down, the server not reached); only a question the server can't use says why in plain words ("That message is too long. Shorten it or split it in two.", which no longer says "LLM"). A reply that couldn't be spoken says "I couldn't say this one aloud, so here it is as text." without the cause. The causes go to the browser console and the server log as before. The question box, buttons and search box have a clear focus ring for keyboard use; the status line is read out by screen readers and an error is announced.

### R41e. Professional host pages (Medium) · items 24, 26, 48, 50 · Done in [PR #82](https://github.com/larry94555/ImageSkinForLLM/pull/82)
- Setup, Manage Content and the Wiki get the same look as the chat, and their wording is checked so a non-technical host can use them without help: plain words for each button and step, a short line saying what each page is for, and clear progress and done messages.
- No technical words, ids or raw data are shown to the host, apart from the timings that the metrics setting turns on.
- **Can show:** screenshots of each host page, and a walk through adding a PDF, reading its wiki and correcting an entry with nothing explained beforehand.
- **Built:** Setup, Manage Content and the Wiki share the chat's look: a wider page, a short line under each title saying what the page is for, each part in a card, and links and keyboard rings in the accent colour. Setup numbers its parts "Step 1 of 4" to "Step 4 of 4" (Photos, Voice recordings, Accent, Prepare the video), its first button says "Prepare the video", and its progress steps are in plain words ("Make the video of the person listening" rather than "Render the idle video (head movement)"). Manage Content says what happens after a PDF is added or replaced, shows "Wiki ready: 6 topics", and no longer links to the raw data of what was read. Each PDF in the Wiki has its buttons in one row. The pages say "the AI" rather than "the LLM", and the server's messages that reach these pages no longer name the README, pip, the LLM's address or its error: a wiki the AI refused to build, a context window too small, a HEIC photo without its add-on, another accent without the voice add-on, a Prepare that failed unexpectedly, and a refusal with no reason ("That didn't work. Please try again.") are each told in plain words, and the log keeps the details.

### R41e2. A designed look (Medium) · items 48, 50 · Done in [PR #83](https://github.com/larry94555/ImageSkinForLLM/pull/83)
- Added by Larry on 2026-10-14 while testing R41e: the app should look like a site made by a contemporary professional web designer, not an unadorned one made by a technologist, and stay just as easy to use.
- One design across every page: a typeface, a colour palette with one accent, a type scale, rounded cards with soft shadows, buttons that tell the main action from the others, and a dark mode. The layout, the steps and the words stay as they are.
- **Can show:** before and after screenshots of every page on a laptop, a tablet and a phone, and in dark mode.
- **Built:** the Inter typeface (OFL, bundled so it works offline), a cool grey page with white cards, an indigo accent, and rounded buttons: the main action filled, the others white with a border, Remove in red. The top bar stays at the top, frosted, with the app's mark (a speech bubble with eyes), the pages as tabs, and Settings with an icon; on a phone the tabs get a row of their own. The home page has a welcome card and a picture on each choice. The chat has the person in a soft frame with the conversation in a card beside them, questions as indigo bubbles and replies as grey ones. Notes are tinted boxes, and errors red ones. Dark mode has its own colours for all of it.

## Milestone 7b: Knowing what to ask, teach me, quiz me and the assessment

| PR | Description | Status |
