| [R41f. "What can you talk about?"](#r41f-what-can-you-talk-about-medium--items-52-53--done-in-pr-85) | Answers "What can you talk about?" with the content's topics. | Done in [PR #85](https://github.com/larry94555/ImageSkinForLLM/pull/85) |
| [R41g. Topic hints in the chat](#r41g-topic-hints-in-the-chat-simple--item-53--done-in-pr-86) | Topic hints and clickable example questions under the prompt box. | Done in [PR #86](https://github.com/larry94555/ImageSkinForLLM/pull/86) |
| [R41h. Teach me](#r41h-teach-me-medium--item-54--done-in-pr-87) | A Teach me lesson that walks through a topic, or all of them in order. | Done in [PR #87](https://github.com/larry94555/ImageSkinForLLM/pull/87) |
| [R41i. Quiz me](#r41i-quiz-me-medium--item-54--done-in-pr-88) | A Quiz me button that asks and judges questions on a topic. | Done in [PR #88](https://github.com/larry94555/ImageSkinForLLM/pull/88) |
| [R41j. Assessment](#r41j-assessment-medium--item-55--done-in-pr-90) | An assessment across all the content, with an evaluation at the end. | Done in [PR #90](https://github.com/larry94555/ImageSkinForLLM/pull/90) |

### R41f. "What can you talk about?" (Medium) · items 52, 53 · Done in [PR #85](https://github.com/larry94555/ImageSkinForLLM/pull/85)
- A list of the content's topics, taken from the wiki's main entries and named in plain words, kept up to date as PDFs are added, removed or replaced.
- A question like "What topics can you talk about?" is answered from that list, in a short spoken reply that suggests one to ask about, even when general prompts are off (R41), in which case it also says questions outside these topics can't be answered.
- A prompt declined by R41 names a few topics it can answer instead.
- **Can show:** with the guides and the Riemann paper added and general prompts off, ask "What topics can you talk about?" and hear the topics, then ask something off-topic and hear the decline name a few of them.
- **Built:** the topics are each PDF's first-level wiki entries, with the host's edits, read again for every question so they follow the PDFs. "What topics can you talk about?", "What do you know about?", "What can I ask?" and similar questions are answered without the AI: the topics (up to 8, then how many more are on the Wiki page), "I can only answer questions about these topics" when general prompts are off, and one topic to start with, picked at random. The answer is sent to the AI with later questions. Before any PDF is added it says it can talk about anything, and while the PDFs are still being read it says to ask again in a little while. A declined question names three topics, picked at random, to ask about instead.

### R41g. Topic hints in the chat (Simple) · item 53 · Done in [PR #86](https://github.com/larry94555/ImageSkinForLLM/pull/86)
- Under the prompt box, a short line naming a few of the topics, and a few example questions drawn from the wiki that send the question when clicked. When general prompts are off, the line says questions must be about these topics.
- **Can show:** screenshots of the chat with its hints before and after a PDF is added, and an answer to a clicked example question.
- **Built:** the line names the first three topics, then how many more are on the Wiki page: "Questions must be about these topics: …" when general prompts are off, "You can ask me about …" when they are on. While a PDF is still being read or its wiki built, it says "So far I can talk about …" and that the rest is still being read, so a partial list is never given as all that can be asked. Up to three example questions ask about each topic's first part (its first sub-entry on the wiki), or the topic itself when it has none, in turn as "What can you tell me about …?", "Can you explain …?" and "Tell me about ….". Each is looked up as the chat would look it up, and only those that find something are shown, so a clicked example is never declined. Clicking one sends it at once. The hints are read again after every reply, so they follow the PDFs; before any PDF is added there are none.

### R41h. Teach me (Medium) · item 54 · Done in [PR #87](https://github.com/larry94555/ImageSkinForLLM/pull/87)
- A **Teach me** button by the prompt box, also started by typing "teach me about …", starts at the named topic. When none is named, it asks the participant to choose one from the list in R41f, or **Everything, from the start** to teach the topics in order. It explains each topic in short parts from the wiki and PDFs, main ideas first, then the detail. After each part the participant can ask about it, say "next", or ask for it again more simply.
- It goes on for as long as the participant wants: at the end of each topic it gives a one-line summary, offers a quick quiz, and moves on to the next, until all the content is taught. What was covered is kept on that browser, so a participant who stops picks up where they left off. Once accounts exist, R46 keeps it for each signed-in participant instead, and R45 leaves Teach me, Quiz me and the assessment out for anonymous visitors (item 51).
- **Can show:** a lesson on the Riemann paper with a question asked partway through and "say it more simply" used once, the move to the next topic, and picking up again after a reload.
- **Built:** a **Teach me** button under Send, or typing "Teach me", "Teach me about …" or "Teach me everything". Without a topic it asks which one, with a button for each topic, **Everything, from the start**, and **Carry on with …** for the topic left off. A topic is taught in parts: its main ideas (the topic's own wiki entry), then every entry under it in the wiki's order, then a one-line summary, then on to the next topic, until the last one. Each part is written by the AI from that entry and what the PDFs say about it, in two to four sentences. While a lesson is going, the chat shows the topic and part with **Next**, **Say it more simply** and **Stop the lesson** (typing "next", "simpler" or "stop" works too). Anything else typed is a question about the part, answered from its material, and the lesson stays where it is. The browser keeps the topic and part, so after a reload or a stop, Teach me offers to carry on from the same part. With Quiz me on (R41i), the end of each topic offers a quick quiz on it. Teach me is off by default: **Teach me** in the Settings menu turns it on, and while it is off there is no Teach me button and "Teach me" is an ordinary prompt (Larry, 2026-10-15).

### R41i. Quiz me (Medium) · item 54 · Done in [PR #88](https://github.com/larry94555/ImageSkinForLLM/pull/88)
- A **Quiz me** button, also started by typing "quiz me on …", asks one question at a time about a topic, drawn from the wiki and PDFs. Each typed answer is judged right, partly right or wrong, with the correct answer and a one-line reason from the content, in a friendly way. A quiz has 5 questions, can be stopped at any time, and ends with how many were right and what to go over again.
- **Can show:** a quiz on one topic with a right, a partly right and a wrong answer, and the summary at the end.
- **Built:** a **Quiz me** button by Send, or typing "Quiz me", "Quiz me on …" or "Test me on …". Without a topic it asks which one, with a button for each. A quiz has 5 questions on one topic, spread over its parts (its main ideas first, then the entries under it). The AI writes each question from that part's wiki entry and what the PDFs say about it; a question it can't write, one that isn't a question, or one already asked, is asked as "What can you tell me about …?". Each reply is sorted before it is judged (Larry, 2026-10-15): asking for the question again ("What's the question?", "Ask it again") says it again; asking what it means ("Can you explain the question?") explains it in simpler words, without the material so the answer isn't given away, then asks it again; not knowing ("I don't know, just give me the answer") gives the answer, counts it as one to go over again, and moves on. A short reply is sorted by its words when they are clear, and by the AI when its words may be about the quiz but don't settle it; anything else is an answer. Whatever is typed next is the answer: the AI judges it from the same material, starting with "Right!", "Partly right." or "Not quite.", then the correct answer and a one-line reason, and the next question follows in the same reply. It ends with how many were right and partly right, and the parts to go over again. **Stop the quiz** (or typing "stop") ends it with the score so far. The browser keeps the quiz, so a reload doesn't lose it. A quiz going takes the answer before a lesson does, and the end of a lesson's topic offers **Quiz me on …** for it. Quiz me is off by default, like Teach me: **Quiz me** in the Settings menu turns it on. How an answer is judged (open question R41i) is the simplest option: the chosen AI, with the question, the passages and the answer; the small local model is lenient, so it can call a partly right answer right.

### R41j. Assessment (Medium) · item 55 · Done in [PR #90](https://github.com/larry94555/ImageSkinForLLM/pull/90)
- An **Assessment** button, also started by typing "assess me", asks a sample of questions across all the content, one at a time. Each question is about one main topic and at one level (recall a fact, explain an idea, or apply or connect ideas), and counts toward both. There are 15 questions, or one per main topic when there are more topics; every topic gets one before any gets a second, and the levels are split as evenly as possible, so each has at least 5. Answers are judged as in R41i, with the correct answers kept for the end.
- It ends with an evaluation: the overall result, breadth (topics answered well out of all the topics), depth at each level, and what to go over again, with an offer to start Teach me on it.
- **Can show:** an assessment on the guides and the Riemann paper with every topic asked about, and its evaluation; then the same assessment after Teach me on the topics it named, scoring higher.
- **Built:** an **Assessment** button by Send, or typing "Assess me", "Test me on everything" or "Give me an assessment"; off by default, like Quiz me, and turned on with **Assessment** in the Settings menu. It has 15 questions, or one per topic when there are more, up to 200 topics; with more, it says there are too many rather than leave some out. The topics are dealt out in turn, so every topic is asked once before any twice. Each question takes the level (recall, explain, apply) asked least so far that its topic didn't have last time, so the levels are split evenly and a topic asked again moves to another level. A recall question is written from the topic's main ideas, a deeper one from the entries under it, in turn; when the AI can't write one, a plain recall or explain question that restates the material is asked, never one already asked; there is no plain apply question, since the material may not say how it applies, so the AI is asked twice and then the request fails, to be sent again. When a judgement leaves out the correct answer, it is asked for again; if there is still none, the answer isn't taken and can be sent again, so the evaluation always has it. Each answer is judged by the AI from the material its question came from, as "Verdict: …" and "Answer: …", and both are kept for the end: the reply says only "Thanks." and the next question, so nothing is taught as it goes. "What's the question?" hears it again, and "I don't know" counts as wrong with the answer kept. Each reply is sorted before it counts as an answer, by its words when they are clear and by the AI otherwise: asking what the question means gets a short explanation; asking for a hint gets one from the material that shouldn't give the answer; checking a guess ("Was it …?") hears to give it as an answer; a question about the assessment ("How many are left?", "Is it marked?") is answered. All of these keep the same question. Asking for an answer or to talk about a question ("Can you give me the answer to question 1?") stops the assessment, talks about it using the questions' correct answers, and gives the evaluation so far. The evaluation gives the overall result, breadth (topics with no wrong answer, out of all of them), depth (right answers at each level), the correct answer to each question missed, and the topics to go over again, with a **Teach me about …** button for each when Teach me is on. Stopping early gives the evaluation so far. It starts only once all the material has been read. If the topics change in any way (one added, removed, replaced or reordered) it ends, and if a question's material changes it is asked anew, as in Quiz me.

## Milestone 8: Spoken prompts

| PR | Description | Status |
|---|---|---|
| [R24. Push-to-talk microphone](#r24-push-to-talk-microphone-medium--item-9--done-in-pr-91) | Hold a microphone button to ask a question out loud. | Done in [PR #91](https://github.com/larry94555/ImageSkinForLLM/pull/91) |
| [R179. Check words perhaps misheard](#r179-check-words-perhaps-misheard-simple--item-9--done-in-pr-214) | A question the browser isn't sure it heard right waits in the prompt box to be fixed and sent; one heard clearly is sent at once; Edit, while listening, stops without sending. | Done in [PR #214](https://github.com/larry94555/ImageSkinForLLM/pull/214) |

### R24. Push-to-talk microphone (Medium) · item 9 · Done in [PR #91](https://github.com/larry94555/ImageSkinForLLM/pull/91)
- Microphone button, push-to-talk, transcribed with browser speech recognition (whisper.cpp can be added later as a separate PR if needed); the transcribed prompt is shown as sent.
- **Can show:** hold the button, ask a question out loud, and the person answers on video.
- **Built:** a **Hold to talk** button by Send. Holding it (with the mouse, a finger, or the space bar or Enter) listens with the browser's own speech recognition, showing the words in the prompt box as they are heard; letting go sends them as if typed. Speech recognition doesn't know the content's names and terms (it heard "Euler" as "Oiler" or "Boiler"), so before a spoken question is answered, the AI is given the content's own names and terms (from its titles, and the capitalised words of its summaries) and puts back any word it misheard; the change is kept only when each word it puts in is one of those and is spelled like the word it replaces, and the question then shows as it was answered. A question about something in the article ("What did Euler notice?") is no longer refused as off topic because its asking words ("notice", "explain") aren't in the content; they no longer count against a passage that lacks them. It keeps listening for 0.6 seconds after the button is let go, so the last word isn't cut off. Asked for an example ("Can you give me an example?"), the AI gives one or two particular ones for the question before it, from the content when it has them; and a long paragraph is cut to the part with the question's words, so the paragraph naming Euler shows him. Where the browser has no speech recognition (Firefox), the button isn't shown. Nothing heard, a microphone the browser may not use, or no microphone each get a plain message. The browser console has `Push-to-talk: listening`, `Push-to-talk: heard N words in N ms` and `Push-to-talk failed`.

### R179. Check words perhaps misheard (Simple) · item 9 · Done in [PR #214](https://github.com/larry94555/ImageSkinForLLM/pull/214)
- Added by Larry on 2026-10-28 while testing PR #213: Chrome heard "Jill Biden" as "Joe Budden" or "Bill Biden", and Hold to talk sent it at once. When a word may be wrong, the question should wait for him to fix it and press Send; when it is clearly heard, it is still sent at once.
- **Built:** the browser's speech recognition says how sure it is (0 to 1) of each phrase it hears; it doesn't say it for each word. When it is less sure than `SURE` (0.8) of any phrase, letting go of Hold to talk sends nothing: what was heard stays in the prompt box, the cursor is put there, and the chat shows "I am not clear on what you said. Can you edit your question and resend?" as the reply (Larry, reviewing PR #214), without adding it to the conversation. Sent unchanged, it is still a spoken question, so R24 puts back misheard names; changed, it is sent as typed. When the browser gives no confidence (0, as Chrome does for a phrase still changing, or none), it is sent as before. While it listens, the button says "Let go to send or…" (or "Click to send or…" after a tap), no longer starting with "Listening…", and an **Edit** link shows just right of it (Larry, reviewing PR #214): clicking Edit, or letting go of a held button over it (the button then says "Let go to edit" and Edit is underlined), stops listening and sends nothing, leaving what was heard in the box to change and send. The browser console has `Push-to-talk: sent (confidence N)`, `Push-to-talk: not sure of the words (confidence N), left to check` or `Push-to-talk: Edit chosen; left in the box to change`.

## Milestone 8a: The default people, made and packed

| PR | Description | Status |
|---|---|---|
| [R24a. Default person test](#r24a-default-person-test-medium--items-57-58--done-in-pr-96) | A test making a few default people and checking they pass the photo checks and animate. | Done in [PR #96](https://github.com/larry94555/ImageSkinForLLM/pull/96) |
| [R24a2. More varied default people test](#r24a2-more-varied-default-people-test-medium--items-57-60--done-in-pr-97) | A test of a second, more varied face for each default person. | Done in [PR #97](https://github.com/larry94555/ImageSkinForLLM/pull/97) |
| [R24b. The default pictures](#r24b-the-default-pictures-medium--item-57--done-in-pr-98) | All 36 default people made, prepared and packed as downloads. | Done in [PR #98](https://github.com/larry94555/ImageSkinForLLM/pull/98) |
| [R24b2. More varied default people](#r24b2-more-varied-default-people-medium--items-57-60--done-in-pr-112) | 24 more default people with a wider range of looks, 60 in all. | Done in [PR #112](https://github.com/larry94555/ImageSkinForLLM/pull/112) |
| [R24b4. Only adults as default people](#r24b4-only-adults-as-default-people-medium--items-57-60--done-in-pr-165) | Makes every default person an adult who looks 18 or over. | Done in [PR #165](https://github.com/larry94555/ImageSkinForLLM/pull/165) |

### R24a. Default person test (Medium) · items 57, 58 · Done in [PR #96](https://github.com/larry94555/ImageSkinForLLM/pull/96)
- Make a few default pictures, photoreal and animated, male and female, for different roles, with the tool picked for this PR, and check that each passes the photo checks and animates with the photoreal engine. Pick a ready-made Kokoro voice for each gender and accent (Kokoro has American and British voices, male and female). Record the exact voice names chosen and, for each, where it came from and its license, attribution and use terms, in the docs.
- **Can show:** sample videos of photoreal and animated default people, each speaking in both voices for their gender (American and British), for Larry to judge before the rest is built, as R25a did for the voice.
- **Result (PR #96, 2026-10-16):** `experiments/default_person/default_person.py` made 8 people with Segmind SSD-1B on the CPU (photoreal and animated, male and female, as Professor, Boss, Parent and Teenager), and all 8 passed the photo checks, prepared, and talked in both voices: 16 videos, none failed, with the animated faces found like the photoreal ones. Each picture took about 11 minutes and each prepare about 5 on a 4-core CPU. The voices are `af_heart`, `am_michael`, `bf_emma` and `bm_george`; their source, license and terms are in the experiment's README. The pictures are kept in `resources/default-people/`. For R24b: the photoreal faces are turned 15 to 18 degrees (the limit is 25), and all 8 came out white and look alike in age, so its prompts should ask for a range of skin tones, ages and hair.

### R24a2. More varied default people test (Medium) · items 57, 60 · Done in [PR #97](https://github.com/larry94555/ImageSkinForLLM/pull/97)
- Make a second face for each of R24a's eight people, each of a different heritage and skin tone with hair to suit, from the same prompts with a short description of the look added, and check that each passes the photo checks and animates in both voices, as R24a did, since a face finder can miss darker or stylized faces. Only the prompts describe the look; file names and the test page give each face a number.
- **Can show:** a contact sheet of R24a's eight beside the new eight, and the new eight talking in both voices.
- **Result (PR #97, 2026-10-17):** `default_person.py --varied` made a second face for each of R24a's eight people (Black, East Asian, South Asian, Latino, Middle Eastern, Southeast Asian and Indigenous, with R24a's white faces as the first), and all 8 passed the photo checks (scores 82 to 95), prepared and talked in both voices: 16 videos, none failed, with the darker and the animated faces found like the rest. Each picture took about 9 minutes and each prepare about 4 on a 4-core CPU. The look must come first in the prompt: placed after the style and framing, as R24a's prompts are built, SSD-1B ignored it and made a white face again. The pictures and a contact sheet of all 16 are in `resources/default-people/`.

### R24b. The default pictures (Medium) · item 57 · Done in [PR #98](https://github.com/larry94555/ImageSkinForLLM/pull/98)
- All 36 pictures (each gender, realism and role), with R24a2's range of looks spread across them, made and prepared ahead of time, with a script that makes them again, and each person's picture and prepared frames packed as one download. Each pack must be at most 50 MB, so it downloads in 20 seconds or less at 25 Mbit/s; a pack over that is made smaller (fewer or smaller frames) before the PR is done. Before a person is packed, the script checks them: the picture must pass every photo check (item 2), and a short line must prepare, render and animate (mouth moving with the speech, idle loop playing) without error. A person that fails is made again or not shipped, so all 36 are checked, not a sample.
- **Can show:** the script's report with all 36 passing the photo checks and the render check, a contact sheet of all 36 for a human look, the idle loop of a few, and every pack's size within the 50 MB budget.
- **Built:** `tools/default_people/make_default_people.py` makes, checks and packs the people listed in `imageskin.default_people` (`people()`, with a face number so R24b2 only adds entries). Each prompt puts the person first, with one of nine looks spread so that each role's four people all look different and every look appears in each gender and realism, and every prompt fits SSD-1B's 77 tokens. A picture failing the photo checks is made again with the next seed, up to 3. The render check speaks a line in the American voice and needs the mouth to move at least 1.5 times as much talking as idling. A pack keeps only the idle loop's 50 rendered frames and the mouth and eye shapes as PNGs; `default_people.unpack` fills in the rest as prepare does, so a 165 MB library packs to about 28 MB and unpacks bit for bit the same in about 3 seconds. **Result (4-core cloud CPU, 2026-10-17):** all 36 passed on the first seed (photo scores 79 to 92), the mouth moved 2.9 to 5.5 times as much talking as idling, and the packs are 22.3 to 32.2 MB, 0.97 GB in all. Each picture took 9 to 10.5 minutes and each prepare 4 to 5.6, 8.5 hours for the whole run. The packs were to be hosted as assets of a GitHub release of this repository; since 2026-10-19 they are hosted on the Oracle free-tier machine instead, in R32a.

### R24b2. More varied default people (Medium) · items 57, 60 · Done in [PR #112](https://github.com/larry94555/ImageSkinForLLM/pull/112)
- 24 more people, 60 in all with R24b's 36 (at most 60 for now, Larry, 2026-10-17), made, checked and packed by R24b's script exactly as its 36 are, each as a second face for one of the 36 choices: six for each gender and realism, spread over the roles. R24b2 picks the 24 to fill what R24b's 36 lack, so that across the 15 people of each gender and realism (Larry, 2026-10-17):
  - **Heritage and hair:** every one of the eight heritages R24a and R24a2 test (white, Black, East Asian, South Asian, Latino, Middle Eastern, Southeast Asian and Indigenous) appears, and at least one has red hair and one blond hair.
  - **Glasses:** at least three wear ordinary clear glasses (no tint or glare, so the photo checks pass, item 2).
  - **Older people:** at least three are in their 60s or 70s, in roles where that fits (Professor, Parent, Boss, Office Manager, High School Teacher and Business Consultant). Teenager, Student and Teaching Assistant stay young.
  - **Visible disability:** at least two show a disability that can be seen in a head-and-shoulders picture without covering the face: sitting in a wheelchair with its back and handles in view, or wearing a hearing aid or a cochlear implant.
- The counts are measured across all 15 people of each gender and realism once R24b2 is done, and one person may count toward several of them (for example, an older Professor with glasses and a hearing aid). Since R24b's 36 add no glasses or disabilities, those come from the six new faces in each group.
- Only the prompts name the heritage or look; files and the app give each face a number. The new looks (glasses, wheelchair, hearing aid) are made and checked first, since R24a2 tested none of them; a look that keeps failing the photo checks is reworded until it passes. At about 17 minutes a person on a 4-core CPU, the 24 take about 7 hours, run once ahead of time.
- **Disk space:** at about 28 MB a pack (R24b's first person), the 60 packs come to about 1.7 GB (at most 3 GB at the 50 MB limit), kept as assets of the GitHub release R24b picks, not in the repository; the pictures add about 75 MB. While making them, each prepared person takes about 165 MB before packing, about 4 GB for the 24. A user's machine keeps only the people they choose (about 200 MB each, unpacked) and the small pictures that ship with the app (R24c2).
- The photo checks can't see these looks, and SSD-1B can leave part of a prompt out (R24a2), so each of the 60 pictures is looked at and the report records which looks it actually shows, not just what its prompt asked for. A person missing a look they are counted for is remade or reworded until every count is met by the pictures that ship.
- **Can show:** the script's report with all 24 passing the photo checks and the render check, a table of the looks each of the 60 pictures shows with every group's counts met, a contact sheet of all 60 for Larry to confirm the table, and every pack within the 50 MB budget.
- **Built:** `SECOND_FACES` in `default_people.py` gives each gender and realism the same six: three older people (one with glasses, one in a wheelchair, one Indigenous), a young person in a wheelchair and two more with glasses, over different roles and heritages. SSD-1B drew no hearing aid, cochlear implant or white cane in any try, and left a wheelchair out of a head-and-shoulders picture, so both visible disabilities are wheelchairs, described first in the look with no head-and-shoulders framing. `tools/default_people/looks_shown.json` records what each of the 60 pictures was seen to show, the report counts it for each group, and a unit test fails if a group falls short. The script also writes a 128 by 128 thumbnail of each person for R24c2 (456 KB for all 60, in `web/public/default-people/`). **Result (4-core cloud CPU, 2026-10-18):** all 24 passed the photo checks (scores 77 to 91), the mouth moved 2.7 to 5.1 times as much talking as idling, and the packs are 22.5 to 31.4 MB (1.6 GB for all 60); every group of 15 meets every count. Five use a seed other than 1, three of them wheelchair people whose seed was picked from quick drafts.

### R24b4. Only adults as default people (Medium) · items 57, 60 · Done in [PR #165](https://github.com/larry94555/ImageSkinForLLM/pull/165)
- Added by Larry on 2026-10-24: every default person must be 18 or over and look it. The 10 Teenager people were asked for "aged sixteen", and four more looked sixteen or seventeen at their role's age: `animated_female_student`, `animated_male_student`, `photoreal_nonbinary_student` and `photoreal_nonbinary_teaching_assistant`.
- **Built:** the Teenager role is now Young Adult (`ROLES` in `default_people.py`), so its 10 people are `*_young_adult*`, and `LOOKED_YOUNG` asks for the other four older. All 14 are asked for as an "adult" woman, man or androgynous person in their late twenties in a button-up shirt; quick drafts of "young" or "mid twenties" in a hoodie still looked like teenagers, and the first full animated pictures looked about 18, so the animated women and men are asked for in their early thirties, the photoreal nonbinary people in their late thirties and the animated nonbinary person in their early forties, all with "child, teenager, childlike face" in the negative prompt. The animated woman in a wheelchair was drawn full length with the face too small on three seeds, so her look asks for waist-up. The young adults keep the younger voices. A saved choice of a Teenager is no longer valid, so that user picks a face again. The four Teenager pictures from R24a are taken out of `resources/default-people/`, and the R24a experiment's Teenager is a Young Adult too.
- **Can show:** a contact sheet of the 14 new faces beside the old ones, every one passing the photo checks and the render check, every pack within 50 MB, the looks counts still met, and a unit test that no role, name or prompt is a teenager's.

### R27. Settings page (Medium) · items 8 (part 1), 28 · Done in [PR #100](https://github.com/larry94555/ImageSkinForLLM/pull/100)
- A **Settings** link at the right of the top bar on every screen opens a Settings page, which takes over R41a's Settings menu: the view (R41b), the timing metrics (R41a), Teach me, Quiz me, Assessment and Debug, grouped under View, Chat and Display, and **Return to the app**, back to the page it was opened from. The page is in both views, so a participant view can be switched back.
- The question scope setting from R41 moves here as **Answer questions outside the content**, holding from the next prompt; the config file's `general_prompts` is only its starting value. R27 was split in three on 2026-10-17 (Larry asked for the next PR that doesn't wait on R24b), so each stays Medium or smaller: R27a and R27b add the other options of item 8.
- **Can show:** open Settings from any screen, turn on answers outside the content, ask an off-topic question and see it answered, and return to where you were.
- **Built:** only the settings changed are saved in `ui-settings.json`, so a setting never changed follows its default, which for the question scope is the config file's. The log has `Question scope` at start-up with `general_prompts` and `config_general_prompts`, and `Settings changed` with each change; the browser console has `Settings page opened` and `Settings changed` with how long the save took.

### R27a. Your photos and recordings in Settings (Medium) · item 8 (part 1) · Done in [PR #101](https://github.com/larry94555/ImageSkinForLLM/pull/101)
- The Settings page lists the photos and recordings in use, to view each photo and play each recording, with **Check again** on each to run its photo or sound checks again (items 2 and 3), showing the result as setup does.
- **Can show:** from Settings, play a recording, view a photo, and check both again.
- **Built:** the page shows the photo the video is made from and the recordings that passed the checks (the ones in the voice sample), each with **Check again**. What is in use is read again after each check, and a file that drops out is marked so with its result; setup is where a file is added, removed or chosen. A check that can't run, because the checks are off or fail, is an error, never the saved result. The log has `Upload checked` with `kind`, `upload`, `problems` and `check_ms`, or `Upload could not be checked`; the browser console has `Photos and recordings in use` and `Checked again` with how long the check took.

### R27b. Run the video test again and clear conversation (Simple) · item 8 (part 1) · Done in [PR #103](https://github.com/larry94555/ImageSkinForLLM/pull/103)
- **Run the video test again** renders the sample video again and plays it, and **Clear conversation** clears the chat after asking to confirm. R169 later offers to save first.
- **Can show:** rerun the sample from Settings and watch it, then clear the conversation and return to an empty chat.
- **Built:** both are under **Video and conversation** on the Settings page. The sample is made again from the prepared face and voice and replaces the one there only if nothing was prepared or changed meanwhile; it waits for a reply being made, and replies wait for it, and Prepare and another accent are refused until it is done. It renders the same script from the same photo and voice, so the sample keeps its acceptance (R14) and the chat stays open. Clearing also removes the replies' videos: those being made are stopped and never published, and those made are removed. Clearing also drops the summary of older turns, so the next prompt starts afresh. The log has `Rendering the sample video again`, `Rendered the sample video again` with `duration_s` (or `Could not run the video test again`) and `Conversation cleared` with `turns`; the browser console has `Video test run again` and `Conversation cleared` with how long each took.

### R28. Changing photos, voice or accent (Medium) · item 8 (part 2) · Done in [PR #105](https://github.com/larry94555/ImageSkinForLLM/pull/105)
- Change image files, change sound files, and switch accent from Settings.
- Any of these requires revalidation and a newly accepted sample before chat resumes; "Goodbye" and "Welcome back" are re-rendered on each new acceptance.
- **Can show:** swap a photo from Settings, get sent through validation and a new sample, then return to the chat.
- **Built:** a **Photo, voice and accent** section on the Settings page (host view only, since setup is the host's) says whether the chat is open or locked, with **Change photos**, **Change recordings** and **Change accent**, which open setup at that part (`#/setup/photos`, `#/setup/sounds`, `#/setup/accent`) with what to do there. The server already treated any change of the chosen photo, the recordings or the accent as a new sample needing acceptance, and Prepare makes the sample, Goodbye and Welcome back together, so they are made again before the new sample is accepted. The log has `Accepted sample is no longer current` with `changed` (`not prepared`, `photo_id`, `voice_id` or `prepared again`); the browser console has `Changing the <part> from Settings`, `Setup opened to change the <part>` and `Sample accepted: <true or false> (N ms)`.

### R29. Exit and Start again (Simple) · items 13, 14 · Done in [PR #106](https://github.com/larry94555/ImageSkinForLLM/pull/106)
- Exit plays "Goodbye." and shows the exit screen; Start again plays "Welcome back." and keeps history.
- **Can show:** exit, then start again with the conversation still there.
- **Built:** **Exit** is the last button under the person on the chat page, in both views. A reply being spoken is stopped first, as Stop does, then the person says "Goodbye." (the clip Prepare made with the sample) and the exit screen shows "Goodbye" with **Start again**, which goes back to the chat: the conversation is the server's, so it is still there, and the person says "Welcome back." A prompt still being answered at Exit is waited for before the conversation is read again, so it is in it (review on PR #106). Nothing can be sent, said with Hold to talk or exited while either line is said. A line whose video isn't there, can't be played or hasn't ended after 10 seconds is passed over, so Exit and Start again never get stuck. The log has `Serving a fixed line` with `clip` (`goodbye` or `welcome-back`); the browser console has `Exit`, `Saying Goodbye`, `Said Goodbye (N ms)`, `Exit screen shown`, `Start again`, `Waiting for the prompt asked before Exit` and `Said Welcome back (N ms)`.

### R30. Saved setup and history (Medium) · item 19 · Done in [PR #108](https://github.com/larry94555/ImageSkinForLLM/pull/108)
- The prepared face, voice and chat history are saved after acceptance and restored on a return visit, so setup is skipped.
- **Can show:** close the browser, come back, and go straight to the chat with history intact.
- **Built:** the prepared face and voice and the acceptance were already saved in the data folder (`prepare.json`, `photoreal`, `clips` and the review), so what was missing was the conversation, kept only in the server's memory. It is now saved in `conversation.json` in the data folder after each reply, summary and clear (everything said, the turns sent word for word and the summary of older ones), written to a new file first so a stopped server never leaves a broken one, and read back when the server starts, so the LLM still remembers it. A file that can't be read is logged and a new conversation starts; a save that fails is logged and the chat goes on. A visit that opens the app's address (http://localhost:8000/) goes straight to the chat once a sample is accepted; Home from the top bar still shows the choices. The log has `Conversation restored` with `turns`, `summary` and `duration_ms` at start-up (or `No saved conversation; starting a new one`), and `Conversation saved` with `turns` and `duration_ms`; the browser console has `Setup is saved, so the visit starts in the chat`.

### R31. Delete my data (Simple) · item 23 · Done in [PR #109](https://github.com/larry94555/ImageSkinForLLM/pull/109)
- One action in Settings deletes uploads, the prepared face and voice, and history, then returns to the start of setup.
- **Can show:** delete everything and confirm in the logs and on disk that nothing remains.
- **Built:** **Delete my data** under **Your data** on the Settings page (host view only) asks first, then clears the conversation and its videos as Clear conversation does and removes `uploads`, `photoreal`, `clips`, `replies`, `prepare.json`, `conversation.json`, `review.json`, `accent.json` and `consent.json` from the data folder, and setup opens at consent. The models, the PDFs and their wiki, and the settings are kept, as they aren't the person's (R31a deletes the PDFs and their wiki too). It is refused while setup is preparing, with nothing removed; a file that can't be removed (open elsewhere, as on Windows) is logged and counted, and clicking again removes it. The log has `Deleting my data` and `Deleted my data` with `files`, `bytes`, `left` and `duration_ms`; the browser console has `Deleted my data: N file(s)`.

### R31a. Delete my data includes the PDFs (Simple) · item 23 · Done in [PR #164](https://github.com/larry94555/ImageSkinForLLM/pull/164)
- Delete my data also deletes the PDFs and everything made from them: what was read, the wiki and its outline, the edits and saved versions, the lessons written ahead (R98a) and the build pace (Larry, 2026-10-24: there was no easy way to delete all the PDF content).
- **Can show:** add a PDF and let its wiki build, delete my data, and see Manage Content and the wiki empty and no `content` folder left in the data folder.
- **Built:** before the files are removed, each PDF is removed as Manage Content's **Remove** does, so a reading or wiki build of it stops and saves nothing more; then `content` goes with the rest of the person's data in `delete_data.py`. A PDF that can't be removed (open elsewhere) is listed as left, and clicking again removes it. A Prepare that refuses the delete keeps the PDFs too. Codex's review: a PDF being read stops at its next page and lets its private copy go before the folder is removed (it waits up to 10 seconds), and the lessons written ahead are forgotten, so a part the LLM is still writing, or the round the removal asks for, saves nothing afterwards; the wiki's build pace isn't saved for a removed PDF either. Codex's second review: adding, replacing or removing a PDF and changing its wiki (edits, rebuild, restore, loading a wiki file) take the same lock and delete count as the person's files, so one under way finishes before the delete and is deleted with the rest, and one that waited for the delete is refused with nothing written; no consent is needed for them. The confirmation lists "Every PDF, with its wiki, edits, saved versions and lessons" and no longer says the PDFs are kept. Only the models and the settings are kept now. The log has `PDF reading stopped` (if one was being read), `Lessons forgotten` and `PDFs forgotten` with `pdfs` and `duration_ms`, then `Deleted my data`.

### R33. Cloud LLMs with the user's key (Medium) · item 16 (later) · Done in [PR #111](https://github.com/larry94555/ImageSkinForLLM/pull/111)
- OpenAI by config, and a small Claude adapter; the user's API key is set in the config file, on the server only. Entering it on the Settings page moved to R42, which adds the LLM setting there (2026-10-18).
- **Can show:** the same conversation answered by Claude or OpenAI instead of local llama.cpp.

### R42. LLM setting with API keys (Medium) · item 29 · Done in [PR #115](https://github.com/larry94555/ImageSkinForLLM/pull/115)
- A setting that picks the LLM: local llama.cpp (the default), or Claude, OpenAI, Grok or OpenRouter with an API key entered on the Settings page, building on R33's Claude and OpenAI support. Keys stay on the server. The LLM choice and key are set only on the Settings page: R42 removes R33's `llm_provider` and `llm_api_key` from the config file (Larry, 2026-10-19).
- **Can show:** the same question answered by the local model and by each provider with a key.
- **Built:** an **LLM** section on the Settings page (host view) picks Local (llama.cpp), Claude, OpenAI, Grok or OpenRouter, with an API key and a model for each provider, picked from the list the provider gives for the key, looked up live since a fixed list goes out of date (Larry, 2026-10-19), or typed (blank for `claude-opus-5-5`, `gpt-4.1-mini`, `grok-4` or `openrouter/auto`). `imageskin.llm_choice` saves the choice and each provider's key and model as `llm.json` in the data folder, readable by the owner only where the system allows, and never logs a key or sends it back to the browser. `SwitchableLlm` is the one client the chat, the wiki and its outline use, so a change holds from the next request. Grok and OpenRouter are asked through R33's OpenAI-compatible client. A config file that still has `llm_provider` or `llm_api_key` says they moved to the Settings page.

### R43. Sign in with ChatGPT (Medium) · item 29 · Done in [PR #117](https://github.com/larry94555/ImageSkinForLLM/pull/117)
- Use a ChatGPT subscription instead of an API key: the decision before this PR found it is the only one an app may use (Claude's and Grok's can't be). Split in two on 2026-10-19 to stay Medium: this PR signs in, R43a answers through the plan.
- **Can show:** signing in with ChatGPT on the Settings page, which then names the connected account.
- **Built:** a **ChatGPT plan** section on the Settings page (host view) with **Continue with ChatGPT**. `imageskin.chatgpt_signin` does OpenAI's OAuth sign-in with PKCE: the server sends the browser to auth.openai.com, which registers the app on the first sign-in and sends the browser back to `http://127.0.0.1:<port>/auth/callback`; the server swaps the code for the tokens, checks the identity's signature against OpenAI's published keys and then its claims, and saves them in `chatgpt.json` in the data folder, readable by the owner only where the system allows. The page is told only the account and whether it allowed plan usage. **Disconnect** revokes the tokens, trying again with backoff after a network failure or 5xx while they are still saved, and then forgets them; the app's registration is kept, so signing in again registers nothing new, and a returning sign-in that comes back for another app or as another account is refused; **Use another ChatGPT account** registers anew while each earlier account keeps its own registration, tokens and ID token (switched to with no sign-in while still connected, or signed in again with `id_token_hint` while still connected; Disconnect ends only the account in use and forgets its tokens and ID token; each registration has a saved label, its email numbered when another shares it), nothing is saved until a sign-in's code swap and identity check pass (except that a new registration whose code is refused with `invalid_grant` keeps its issued client ID as `provisional`, and the next new sign-in retries with it), **Allow use of my plan** asks for consent again, and a Disconnect OpenAI didn't confirm says so. Every sign-in sends the same host ID (`ext_agent_host_id`), made once and kept in `chatgpt.json`.

### R43a. Answers through the ChatGPT plan (Medium) · item 29 · Done in [PR #118](https://github.com/larry94555/ImageSkinForLLM/pull/118)
- A **ChatGPT plan** choice in the LLM setting, answered through OpenAI's Responses API with the signed-in account's token (renewed when it expires), and its models listed from the plan's own list.
- **Can show:** a question answered through a ChatGPT subscription.
- **Built:** **ChatGPT plan** is a sixth choice under **LLM** on the Settings page, marked "signed in" or "needs a ChatGPT sign-in"; it has no key box. `imageskin.chatgpt_plan.ChatGptPlanClient` asks `https://api.openai.com/v1/responses` with the account's access token, as OpenAI requires for a plan: streamed and unstored, the system prompt as `instructions`, no token limit or temperature (a wiki's JSON is asked for as a strict `json_schema` text format). `ChatGptSignIn.access_token` renews the token through the token endpoint (`grant_type=refresh_token` with the issued client ID and `resource`) when it ends within a minute, and once more when OpenAI refuses it, one renewal at a time; Disconnect and a sign-in's save wait for a renewal under way, so Disconnect revokes the refresh token it brought; a plan request holds its token until it is sent (`plan_session`), so Disconnect waits for one being sent and none is sent after it (reviews on PR #118). Only `response.completed` ends an answer: `response.incomplete` is an error, so a part reply isn't kept (review on PR #118). A refresh token OpenAI refuses for good (`invalid_grant`, `invalid_refresh_token`, `token_expired`, `refresh_token_expired`, `refresh_token_invalidated`, `refresh_token_reused`) is forgotten, so the page asks for a sign-in; `invalid_client` and `unauthorized_client` say the app's registration was refused and keep the tokens, and other failures keep them too. OpenAI's plan error codes, before or during a stream, say what to do (a plan at its limit, a plan that can't be used here, a route the app sent that a plan doesn't take, a part of the request a plan can't take named by `error.param`, a grant the app's setup lacks, a plan service down for now (either code, or a 503 before the stream with no code), which is tried again after 1 s and 2 s, and an account OpenAI won't take even after a renewal, which keeps the sign-in and gives the request ID), read from the whole error body however long; a 403 alone no longer blames the model. The plan's model list is fetched with the sign-in held too, so none is fetched after Disconnect (reviews on PR #118). The plan's list shows each model's `display_name` (kept with that account's list, not shared between accounts) and sends its `slug`, only for `visibility` "list". Switching ChatGPT accounts reads the LLM setting and the new account's models again, and a saved model the new account doesn't list is replaced with its first (reviews on PR #118). The models are the plan's own list (`GET /v1/models` with the token, those with `visibility` "list", in its order); a blank model saves the first, and a list that can't be had asks for a typed name. A sign-in that has ended, a plan at its limit (429) or a model it can't use says so in the chat. The request shape follows OpenAI's Sign in with ChatGPT docs as reported on 2026-10-19 (this session couldn't open them); a real request needs Larry's ChatGPT account.

## Milestone 10: Hosted

| PR | Description | Status |
|---|---|---|
| [R32. Hosted deployment](#r32-hosted-deployment-medium--deployment--done-in-pr-120) | A container image and step-by-step guide to host the app on Oracle Cloud's free tier. | Done in [PR #120](https://github.com/larry94555/ImageSkinForLLM/pull/120) |

### R32. Hosted deployment (Medium) · Deployment · Done in [PR #120](https://github.com/larry94555/ImageSkinForLLM/pull/120)
- Container image and deployment guide for an HTTPS host, with secrets kept on the server, set up step by step on Oracle Cloud's free tier (Larry, 2026-10-20).
- Access restricted to the one user by the mechanism decided before this PR.
- **Can show:** the full app running on a hosted HTTPS URL, with the microphone working.
- **Built:** the `Dockerfile` makes one image of the app with every voice and video extra (Ubuntu 24.04, Python 3.12, the CPU-only PyTorch, Chatterbox installed as the README does), for x86-64 and for ARM; the uploads, settings, API keys and models are kept in a `/data` volume. `deploy/compose.yaml` runs it with llama.cpp's `llama-server` and the laptop's Gemma 3 4B in a second container (2 threads, as R22a set), behind Caddy, which gets the Let's Encrypt certificate by itself and asks for one name and password (`basic_auth`, the decision's simplest option) before anything is proxied; the password's hash is in `deploy/.env`, on the server only. The app is published only on the server's own `127.0.0.1:8000`, so Sign in with ChatGPT works through an SSH tunnel. `deploy/README.md` sets it up on an Always Free Ampere machine (4 cores, 24 GB), from the account to the first chat, with the laptop's commands for macOS, Linux and Windows, and a free `sslip.io` address when there is no domain. Tested in a cloud session with the address `localhost` (Caddy's own certificate): 401 without the password or with a wrong one, the app with it, `isSecureContext` and the microphone API there; this session couldn't reach Oracle or PyTorch's CPU index, so the real site is Larry's test.

### R24c. Start now with a default person (Medium) · items 56, 58 · Done in [PR #113](https://github.com/larry94555/ImageSkinForLLM/pull/113)
- **Start now with a default person** on the first setup screen, with the four choices (the roles shown as Father or Mother and Son or Daughter by gender); it downloads only the chosen person the first time, with progress and **Try again** if it fails, then skips consent, the uploads, prepare, the sample video and review, and goes to the chat with that picture and voice. Goodbye and Welcome back are spoken in the default voice.
- **Can show:** from a fresh start, choose an animated British female Professor and ask a question; the answer is spoken within the usual time. The first download, throttled to 25 Mbit/s, finishes in 20 seconds or less. Choose the same person again and the chat is ready at once; choose a new one with the network off and see the plain message and **Try again**.
- **Built:** `imageskin.start_now` downloads the chosen person's pack from the release `default-people-v1`, checks it against its size and SHA-256 in `default_people_packs.json`, unpacks it with R24b's `unpack`, then renders Goodbye and Welcome back in the person's Kokoro voice. The choice is saved as `default-person.json`; while it is there, consent and review are not asked for, and the chat, its photo and its voice are the default person's (`PresetVoice`). Accepting a sample of the user's own makes the person theirs again, and Delete my data forgets the choice. **Start now** stands beside **Start setup** on the home page and opens `#/start-now`. The release can't be made from a cloud session; R32a hosts the packs on the Oracle machine instead and points the download there. **Result (4-core cloud CPU, 2026-10-19, packs served from a local folder):** an animated British female Professor was ready 16.8 seconds after **Start now**, the first time: 0.1 s to download 27 MB locally, 1.8 s to unpack, and 13.5 s for Goodbye, most of it Kokoro's first load. Choosing her again opened the chat in 59 ms; a new person with the server stopped said so with **Try again** after 2 seconds.

### R32a. Host the default people (Simple) · item 57 · Done in [PR #122](https://github.com/larry94555/ImageSkinForLLM/pull/122)
- The 60 packs served over HTTPS from the hosted machine R32 sets up, and R24c's download address (`RELEASE_URL` in `start_now.py`) pointed there, with the steps to upload them in `tools/default_people/README.md`. A check downloads every pack from the real address and compares its name, size and SHA-256 with `default_people_packs.json`.
- **Can show:** the check passing for all 60 packs, and, from a fresh start on another machine, an animated British female Professor downloaded from the hosted site and talking, the download within R24c's 20 seconds at 25 Mbit/s.
- **Built:** Caddy serves the `.zip` files in `deploy/default-people` at `https://<site>/default-people/`, before and outside the password, since they are made-up people downloaded by Start now on any computer; anything else there is a 404. The hosted app downloads them from Caddy inside Docker (`http://caddy:8090`, not published), so it doesn't need to reach its own public address. Start now's default address is `https://app.me0.ai/default-people` (`HOSTED_URL`; `IMAGESKIN_DEFAULT_PEOPLE_URL` changes it). `imageskin check-packs [--url]` downloads every pack and compares its size and SHA-256 with `default_people_packs.json`, without keeping it. The packs are copied to the server with `scp`, as `tools/default_people/README.md` says. **Result (cloud session, Caddy 2.10.2 with the site `localhost`, 2026-10-20):** all 60 packs matched over HTTPS (1,520 MB in 75 s), with no password asked for them and 401 for the app without it; Start now got an animated British female Professor from that address in 3.6 s (0.4 s to download, 3.3 s to unpack), and her 27 MB pack took 8.8 s throttled to 25 Mbit/s. On Larry's Oracle machine (2026-10-20), with the packs cloned onto it from a temporary branch of this repository, `imageskin check-packs --url http://caddy:8090` in the app container matched all 60 (1,520 MB in 2 s).

### R24c2. Pick a face (Simple) · item 60 · Done in [PR #124](https://github.com/larry94555/ImageSkinForLLM/pull/124)
- After the four choices, if the choice has two faces, the screen shows both as small pictures, with no label, and the user picks one; the first is picked if they don't. A choice with one face shows it alone. The small pictures are separate thumbnails made from each picture by R24b2's script, 128 by 128 pixel JPEGs of about 10 KB each, under 1 MB for all 60, and ship with the app; after a face is picked, only that person's pack, which holds the full picture and prepared frames, is downloaded (item 57). Changing a choice shows the new choice's faces.
- **Can show:** choose one of the choices R24b2 gave a second face (its report lists them), see the two faces, pick the second, and chat with that person.
- **Built:** the choice carries a face number (1 unless picked), saved with it, and `GET /api/start-now/faces` lists all 60 people; the avatar page shows the faces of the gender, look and role chosen, as the thumbnails, with the first picked and changing a choice going back to its first. Larry's tests (2026-10-21) found one or two faces too few, and then that a Role list tied each role to a look, the opposite of the range's purpose: the range of heritages, ages, glasses and wheelchairs R24b2 made is across each gender and look's 15 people, not each role's. So the Role list is gone: the page shows all 15 people of the gender and look, always in the same order, and the user picks a face; the person's role and face number still name them to the server. The thumbnails moved from `web/public/default-people/` to `web/public/faces/`, since the hosted site keeps `/default-people/` for the packs (R32a) and would answer 404 for them.

### R24c4. No gender to pick (Simple) · items 56, 60 · Done in [PR #136](https://github.com/larry94555/ImageSkinForLLM/pull/136)
- The ready-made avatar page drops its Gender question, as R24c2 dropped Role (Larry, 2026-10-21): after the accent and look, it shows every person of that look, women and men together, always in the same order (by role, then face, then gender, so they take turns), and the user picks a face. The face picked carries its gender, role and face number to the server, which name the person; the gender still picks the voice. This makes room for R24b3's nonbinary people, who then show among the faces with nothing more to choose.
- **Can show:** open Avatar Choice, see no Gender question and 30 faces for Photorealistic, pick a man's face and start, and hear the man's voice.

### R24b3. Nonbinary people (Medium) · items 57, 58, 60 · Done in [PR #139](https://github.com/larry94555/ImageSkinForLLM/pull/139)
- 8 nonbinary people, 68 in all, four for each realism (Larry, 2026-10-21: 18, one for each realism and role, was too many; 6 to 8 is enough), made, checked and packed by R24b's script exactly as the 60 are. Each of the eight heritages appears once across the 8, and each realism has one older person (in a role that suits it) and one with clear glasses, with red hair once. No wheelchair: Larry said it isn't needed for them. Like the others, they show only as pictures on the avatar page (R24c4), among the women and men of their look, with no label.
- **Prompt:** "androgynous person" (or "androgynous teenager") in place of woman or man, with feminine, masculine, makeup, lipstick, earrings, jewelry, beard and moustache in the negative prompt. Quick drafts at 640 by 640 and 12 steps compared four wordings first: the first full picture, asked for as an "androgynous nonbinary person" with the usual negative prompt, read as a woman; "nonbinary" made no visible difference and costs two of the 77 tokens.
- **Voice:** each speaks in an even blend of the accent's woman's and man's Kokoro voice, `af_heart,am_michael` (American) or `bf_emma,bm_george` (British). Kokoro averages the two voices' style vectors when given both names, so no new voice ships. The blend's median pitch is about 167 Hz American and 171 Hz British, between the woman's (207 and 190 Hz) and the man's (123 and 149 Hz), the range heard as neither clearly a woman's nor a man's. They are also offered under **No recordings?** in setup (R24c3).
- **Can show:** the script's report with all 8 passing the photo checks and the render check, a contact sheet of the 8, every pack within the 50 MB budget, and a nonbinary avatar chosen on the avatar page and talking in the blended voice.

### R24c3. A ready-made voice until you add recordings (Simple) · item 59 · Done in [PR #128](https://github.com/larry94555/ImageSkinForLLM/pull/128)
- Recordings are optional in setup: while none has passed the checks (none added, or all rejected or removed), the user's own photo speaks in a ready-made Kokoro voice, the last ready-made avatar's or the one chosen under **No recordings?** in setup, instead of Prepare waiting for recordings (Larry, 2026-10-21). Once recordings pass, the voice is made from them; removing them all brings the ready-made voice back. Either change makes the sample again.
- **Can show:** set up with a photo and no recordings, prepare and accept the sample in the ready-made voice, and chat; then add recordings and prepare again in your own voice.
- **Built:** `ready-voice.json` holds the voice (`af_heart` until an avatar or setup picks one; choosing an avatar saves its voice). While no recording passes, Prepare's voice step keeps it (`voice_id` is `preset:<voice>`) and the replies speak in it. `GET` and `PUT /api/ready-voice` read and choose it, and say whether it is in use. The log has `Ready-made voice chosen` and `Keeping the ready-made voice`; the browser console has `Ready-made voice: <voice>, in use: <true or false>` and `Ready-made voice chosen: <voice> (N ms)`.

### R24d. A ready-made avatar in your own voice (Medium) · item 59 · Done in [PR #131](https://github.com/larry94555/ImageSkinForLLM/pull/131)
- **Use my own voice** under the ready-made avatar in Settings' Avatar Choice keeps the avatar's picture and replaces its ready-made voice with the user's recordings, through the sound checks, the accent, Prepare and review. Consent is asked for the person in the recordings: that they agreed to have their voice cloned. Your own photo with your own voice is already Setup (R14, R28); your own photo with an avatar's voice is not offered, since Larry sees no use for it (2026-10-21, on PR #127, now closed).
- **Can show:** start with a ready-made avatar, choose Use my own voice, add recordings, prepare and accept the sample of the avatar speaking in your voice, and chat with the avatar in your voice.
- **Built:** `default-person.json` gets `own_voice`, set by `PUT /api/start-now/own-voice` with the consent for the person in the recordings (which also opens the recordings, Prepare and review) and cleared by `DELETE` or by choosing an avatar again. While it is set, Prepare prepares the avatar's picture (photo id `avatar-<name>`; its frames came with the pack, so only the voice and the clips are made) with the recordings, never a ready-made voice; once that sample is accepted, the replies, Goodbye and Welcome back are in the user's voice, and until then the avatar keeps its ready-made one with the chat open. Settings' Avatar Choice has **Use my own voice** under the avatar, opening `#/avatar-voice`: the consent, the avatar's picture as step 1, then the recordings, the accent and Prepare as in setup, with **Reject voice** and **Change accent** but no **Reject image**, and **Use the avatar's ready-made voice** to go back. Setup for the user's own photo first offers to undo it, since one Prepare serves one picture. Accepting the avatar's sample no longer switches to the user's own photo. Rated Medium rather than Simple: it needed a page of its own. The log has `Avatar's voice chosen` with `own_voice` and `The avatar speaks in your own voice now`; the browser console has `Avatar to speak in your own voice (N ms)`. Larry's test (2026-10-21): Avatar Choice in Settings has a third choice, **Default voice for the avatar**, whose **Use the Default Voice for the Avatar** switches back at once with nothing to accept (or, with the user's own photo in use, opens the avatars), and the review of the avatar's sample has **Return to default voice for avatar** beside **Accept**, **Reject voice** and **Change accent**, and leaving setup or the avatar's voice page with a sample neither accepted nor rejected asks first, **Proceed without the change** or **Cancel** (`Leaving setup for <page> with the sample neither accepted nor rejected` in the console); a change of the avatar's voice is refused while Prepare runs (Codex's review).
