### R72. Support site and support staff sign-in (Medium) · item 67
- `support.me0.ai` is a reserved site with a first page linking to the guide, the support person, asking the AI, tickets and feedback, open to anyone. Support staff sign in to manage it.
- **Can show:** the first page with its five links, and the staff pages refusing a visitor and opening after sign-in.

### R73. Support guide (Simple) · item 68
- The support guide PDF, written from items 61 to 72, offered to read or download, and added as the support site's content.
- **Can show:** download the guide, and see its wiki built on the support site.

### R74. Support person and asking the AI (Medium) · items 69, 70
- The support site's talking person answers spoken or typed questions from the guide, and **Ask the AI** answers typed questions in text only. When neither can answer, it says so and offers to open a ticket.
- **Can show:** ask "How do I change my subdomain?" and hear the answer from the guide; ask something it doesn't cover and be offered a ticket.

### R75. Opening tickets and the public ticket list (Medium) · item 71
- Anyone opens a ticket with a title and description, after a clear warning that both will be public; a public list shows every ticket with its status, can be searched, and opens each one with its replies. No email address is ever shown. The opener gets a private link to the ticket, the only way to reply as them.
- **Can show:** open a ticket without signing in, after the warning, then find it in the list from another browser.

### R76. Ticket status, staff replies and moderation (Medium) · item 71
- Signed-in support staff change a ticket's status and reply on it; the history of changes shows on the ticket. Staff can hide or delete a ticket or a reply. The opener answers through their private link.
- **Can show:** staff ask a follow-up and set Waiting for reply; the opener answers through their private link, and another browser without the link can read the ticket but not reply. Then move it to Resolved, each change on the ticket and in the logs; hide a spam ticket and see it gone from the public list.

### R77. Email when a ticket changes (Simple) · item 71
- Anyone can give an email address on a ticket to be told when its status changes or the staff reply, starting only after they confirm it from a first email; the opener's emails carry their private link. The address is never shown, and each email has a link to stop them.
- **Can show:** follow a ticket, change its status before confirming and receive nothing, confirm, change it again and receive the email, click stop, change it again and receive nothing.

### R78. Feedback (Simple) · item 72
- A feedback form with an optional email, seen only by support staff on a feedback page.
- **Can show:** send feedback as a visitor and find it on the staff feedback page, not anywhere public.

## Milestone 28: Thinking and quality of response

Added by Larry on 2026-10-29, to build right after the admin view (Milestone 27), before the setup wizard (Milestone 10d); on 2026-10-30 Larry moved it to build right after support at support.me0.ai (Milestone 21), before contexts (Milestone 13). While the app thinks about a prompt, the user sees what it is doing and the thinking is made shorter; each answer is as detailed as its question needs; the topics offered under the prompt box cover every important topic; and Tell Me More goes on with the conversation's topic for as long as the content has more to say. R134c, R134d and R134e moved here from Bug fixing (Milestone 10c) the same day.

| PR | Description | Status |
|---|---|---|
| [R134c. Every step of a long thinking, with its time](#r134c-every-step-of-a-long-thinking-with-its-time-medium--item-120--bug) | Finds and times every step of a long thinking. | Not started |
| [R134d. A model of what is happening at each stage](#r134d-a-model-of-what-is-happening-at-each-stage-simple--item-126--bug) | A set of plain descriptions of what is happening at each stage of thinking. | Not started |
| [R134e. Show what thinking is doing](#r134e-show-what-thinking-is-doing-medium--item-127--bug) | Shows a new description of what thinking is doing every 5 to 10 seconds. | Not started |
| [R184. Shorter thinking](#r184-shorter-thinking-medium--item-144) | Makes the slowest steps of a long thinking, found by R134c, shorter. | Not started |
| [R185. The level of detail detected from the question](#r185-the-level-of-detail-detected-from-the-question-medium--item-145) | Detects from the question how detailed its answer should be. | Not started |
| [R186. Every important topic in the topic list](#r186-every-important-topic-in-the-topic-list-simple--items-53-146) | The topics offered through other topics... cover every important topic in the content. | Not started |
| [R187. Tell Me More](#r187-tell-me-more-medium--item-147) | A Tell Me More button in the chat that goes on with the topic, or moves to a related or more general one. | Not started |

### R134c. Every step of a long thinking, with its time (Medium) · item 120 · Bug
- Added by Larry on 2026-10-27, after testing PR #179. That PR tried to say what thinking is doing while the user waits, and was rolled back: its descriptions didn't change in a way that showed progress, and didn't inform. Its commits stay in the history of the `show-thinking` branch. This PR comes first: find what really happens before describing it.
- Find and time every step between a prompt's arrival and its reply, in full detail: the wait for the replies' warm-up after a start, and each of its steps (on Larry's laptop the first prompt after a start waited 154 seconds, most of it making the voice ready), the wait for another answer or the wiki to finish with the LLM, the summary of the conversation, the search of the PDFs, the LLM reading the prompt and writing the answer, and any other step that takes time. Each step's start, end and duration are logged for every prompt, building on R96's reply timings.
- **Can show:** a slow first prompt after a start and a slow prompt about the PDFs, each broken down step by step with its times, on a default person and on Larry's own photo and voice, with a short report of where the time goes.

### R134d. A model of what is happening at each stage (Simple) · item 126 · Bug
- From R134c's steps and times, a finite set of descriptions: for each stage, a few concise, nontechnical phrases that say what is happening, in an order that shows progress, and what can be measured within it to show how much is done, so a description is informative and never repeats. Reviewed by Larry before R134e applies it.
- **Can show:** the set of descriptions for each stage, next to the step it describes and how long that step takes.

### R134e. Show what thinking is doing (Medium) · item 127 · Bug
- Apply R134d's model to R134c's steps while a prompt is thought about: every 5 to 10 seconds the user sees a new, informative, concise description of what is happening, with a clear sense of progress. The steps and their times stay in the log for the team, to show where the thinking can be made faster.
- **Can show:** a slow prompt with its description changing every 5 to 10 seconds, each one new and showing progress, and the log's breakdown of the same prompt.

### R184. Shorter thinking (Medium) · item 144
- Added by Larry on 2026-10-29. Built after R134c, from its breakdown of where a long thinking's time goes: the slowest steps it finds are made shorter, or taken out of the wait, so the first word of a reply comes sooner, on a default person and on the person's own photo and voice, with every answer as good as before. R146 to R148 shortened the wait for answers from the PDFs; this takes on the rest of what R134c finds, such as the warm-up after a start, waiting for the wiki or another answer, the summary and the search of the PDFs.
- The log keeps R134c's steps and times for every prompt, so the same prompts can be timed before and after.
- **Can show:** the slow prompts of R134c's report, timed before and after step by step, with the time to the first word shorter and the answers unchanged.

### R185. The level of detail detected from the question (Medium) · item 145
- Added by Larry on 2026-10-29. R180's Level of detail bar sets how detailed answers are by hand; this detects from each question the level its answer needs. "What is X?" gets the main points, while "Explain in detail how X works" or "Compare X and Y" gets an answer in depth.
- The bar gets an **Auto** setting, on *(default)*: with it, each answer's level is detected from the question and the conversation so far; moving the bar turns Auto off and the bar's level is used, as in R180. Detecting the level adds no noticeable thinking *(default: under 100 ms, without another request to the LLM)*.
- The log has `Level of detail detected` with the level and what it was detected from, and `Chat prompt sent` the level the answer was given at.
- **Can show:** a set of short and in-depth questions, each answered at the level detected, with the log's level for each, then the bar moved by hand and its level used.

### R186. Every important topic in the topic list (Simple) · items 53, 146
- Added by Larry on 2026-10-29: the topics offered through **other topics...** under the prompt box (R182) must cover every important topic in the content. Today the chat is given up to 24 example questions, each about a topic's first part on the wiki (R41g), so a topic's other important parts, and any topic past the 24, are never offered.
- The list covers every first-level topic of the wiki at least once, then the important parts within each, going round the topics before offering a second part of any, with nothing offered twice. A part is important when it has the most material in the PDFs under its topic *(default)*.
- The log's `Topic hints` line has how many of the wiki's topics and important parts the list covers.
- **Can show:** with several PDFs added, the wiki's topics and important parts next to the topics offered by clicking other topics... until it wraps round, every one of them offered.

### R187. Tell Me More (Medium) · item 147
- Added by Larry on 2026-10-29. A **Tell Me More** button in the chat, beside Repeat and Say it simpler under the latest reply *(default)*, asks for more on the topic or question being discussed. It asks the LLM, so it shows "Thinking…" first, and the new reply is kept in the conversation like any other.
- What comes next, in this order of preference: another part of the same topic not yet discussed; once the topic is exhausted, a related topic; once all related topics have been discussed in detail, a more general topic. A new, relevant reply is always preferred: "That's pretty much it" is said only when everything the PDFs have on the topic, and on related and more general topics, has already been covered in the conversation.
- What has been covered is worked out from the conversation and the wiki's topics and their parts (R41g, R186), so a part already discussed isn't offered again. With no PDFs added, the reply goes on from the LLM's own knowledge in the same order *(default)*.
- The log has `Tell me more` with what was chosen (same topic, related topic, more general topic, or nothing more) and the topic or part it moved to.
- **Can show:** with PDFs added, a question followed by Tell Me More clicked again and again: the replies go through the topic's other parts, then related topics, then more general ones, each new and relevant, with the log's choice for each; and, on a small PDF covered in full, "That's pretty much it".

## Milestone 13: Contexts

| PR | Description | Status |
|---|---|---|
| [R84. What a context is](#r84-what-a-context-is-simple--items-77-79) | Defines a context as data, with Teacher/Student and Meta built in. | Not started |
| [R85. Choosing the context for each prompt](#r85-choosing-the-context-for-each-prompt-medium--items-78-79) | Chooses which context responds to each prompt. | Not started |
| [R86. Which context responded](#r86-which-context-responded-simple--item-82) | With Debug on, shows which context responded to each reply and why. | Not started |
| [R87. Contexts page: which are available](#r87-contexts-page-which-are-available-medium--item-80) | A Contexts page to turn contexts on and off and pick the primary one. | Not started |
| [R88. Changing a context's guidelines and criteria](#r88-changing-a-contexts-guidelines-and-criteria-medium--item-81) | Edit a context's guidelines and criteria. | Not started |
| [R89. Adding a context](#r89-adding-a-context-medium--item-81) | Add a context of your own. | Not started |
| [R90. Criteria described in words](#r90-criteria-described-in-words-medium--item-81) | A context's criteria can be described in words. | Not started |
| [R91. Sample contexts](#r91-sample-contexts-simple--item-83) | Three sample contexts, off by default. | Not started |

The two contexts the app already has:

| Context | Kind | What it does today | Where it is built |
|---|---|---|---|
| Teacher/Student | Primary | Answers questions from the content, with or without general prompts; topic hints; Teach me, Quiz me and the assessment | R39 to R41, R41g to R41j |
| Meta | Supplementary | "What can you talk about?"; in a quiz or assessment: repeat the question, explain what it means, a hint, "I don't know", checking a guess, "How many are left?"; giving examples; a lesson's Next, Say it more simply and Stop | R41f, R41h to R41j, R24 |

Today the order is fixed in the chat's code: an assessment going takes the prompt before a quiz, a quiz before a lesson, and each of them sorts out its own Meta prompts before treating the rest as an answer. R85 keeps exactly that order and those replies, and only names the context and action that responded, so nothing a user sees changes until R87 lets contexts be turned off. Administrator sign-on (R44) is built by then, so the Contexts page is in the host view, which only the signed-on administrator gets.

### R84. What a context is (Simple) · items 77, 79
- A context as data: its name, primary or supplementary, guidelines, criteria (example statements or questions, or a description in words) and whether it is available, kept in `contexts.json` with the other settings. Teacher/Student and Meta ship in it with guidelines that describe what they do today and criteria taken from today's phrasings; Teacher/Student is the primary. Their logic stays the code that is there now.
- `GET /api/contexts` lists them. A file that is missing or can't be read falls back to the shipped contexts, with the error in the log.
- **Can show:** the API listing both built-in contexts with their guidelines and criteria, and the log line when the file is broken and the shipped ones are used.

### R85. Choosing the context for each prompt (Medium) · items 78, 79
- Each prompt goes through one step that chooses the contexts that respond: the available supplementary contexts whose criteria match first, otherwise the primary. The built-in logic moves behind it as it is: the assessment, quiz and lesson keep their own sorting of Meta prompts, and report which context and action responded. A supplementary context responding leaves the primary one where it was, so a quiz question asked again keeps the quiz going. Contexts are chosen before the question scope rule (R41), which becomes part of Teacher/Student's logic: it can decline only a prompt Teacher/Student responds to (item 78).
- No reply changes: every existing chat, Teach me, Quiz me and assessment test passes unchanged. The log has `Context chosen` with the primary, any supplementary context, the action and the time taken.
- **Can show:** the existing tests passing, and the log naming the context and action for an ordinary question, "What can you talk about?", a quiz answer and "What's the question?" during the same quiz.

### R86. Which context responded (Simple) · item 82
- With Debug on in the settings, each reply shows a small line under it with the contexts that responded, the action (for example "Meta: repeat the question, during Teacher/Student: quiz") and why (the criterion that matched, or none, so the primary responded). The copied chat has the same line for each reply.
- **Can show:** a screenshot of a quiz with Debug on, with an answer and a repeated question each showing its line, and the same chat with Debug off showing none.

### R87. Contexts page: which are available (Medium) · item 80
- A **Contexts** page, reached from Settings in the host view, lists each context with its kind, an on/off switch, and a choice of primary among the primary contexts. The list is in order of priority, which decides which supplementary context responds when two match: Meta first, then the samples (R91), then added contexts in the order they were added, with **Move up** and **Move down** on each to change it (item 80). Teach me, Quiz me and Assessment move from the Settings page onto Teacher/Student's entry, keeping their saved values.
- A context turned off is never taken: with Meta off, "What's the question?" in a quiz is judged as an answer and "What can you talk about?" is an ordinary question. The primary can't be turned off.
- **Can show:** turn Meta off, ask "What's the question?" in a quiz and see it judged as an answer; turn it back on and see the question repeated; add two contexts whose criteria share a phrase, ask it, and see the higher one respond, then move the other above it and see it respond instead. The log has `Contexts changed`.

### R88. Changing a context's guidelines and criteria (Medium) · item 81
- On the Contexts page, each context's guidelines and criteria can be edited and saved, and **Set back to how it shipped** restores them. The guidelines of the primary context, and of a supplementary one when it responds, are sent to the LLM with the prompt, so changing them changes how replies are written. In Teacher/Student and Meta that is only the replies the LLM writes (answers, lesson parts, quiz and assessment questions and judgements, explanations, hints and examples); replies made by the code, such as a repeated question, "How many are left?", Next, Stop and the scores, don't change (item 81). The page says so under each built-in context's guidelines. Example criteria are matched like R41f's phrasings; a change takes effect from the next prompt.
- **Can show:** add "Always end with a question to check understanding." to Teacher/Student's guidelines and see answers end with one; add "Say that one more time" to Meta's criteria and see it repeat a quiz question; set both back.

### R89. Adding a context (Medium) · item 81
- **Add a context** on the Contexts page takes a name, primary or supplementary, guidelines and example criteria, and the context can be edited, turned on or off and deleted like the built-in ones (which can't be deleted). Its logic is the same for every added context: its guidelines as instructions to the chosen LLM, with what the content has on the prompt, as answers are (R40). No code is written by the administrator. A prompt it takes is answered even with general prompts off, since the question scope rule is Teacher/Student's (item 78).
- **Can show:** add a supplementary "Out of office" context with criteria "Can I talk to the host?" and guidelines saying the host is away until Monday and how to leave a message; ask that question with general prompts off and get a reply that follows them, with Debug showing the context, while other off-topic questions are still declined.

### R90. Criteria described in words (Medium) · item 81
- A context's criteria can be a description, such as "The visitor wants to buy something or asks about prices", rather than example phrasings. When a context with such criteria is available and no example criterion matched, the chosen LLM is asked once, briefly, which of those contexts the prompt fits, if any. Contexts with only example criteria never cost this call. The call has a timeout equal to the budget decided before this PR; if it times out, fails, or answers with no context or one that isn't on the list, it counts as no match and the primary context responds, so a reply is never held up or lost by it. The log has `Context judged` with the time the call took, or `Context judging failed` with the reason and time, and the Debug line says when it failed.
- **Can show:** a context with criteria described in words taken for three differently worded prompts and not for an unrelated one, with Debug showing why, and the added time in the log within the budget decided before this PR; with the LLM made to stall past the timeout, the primary context answers on time and the log has `Context judging failed`.

### R91. Sample contexts (Simple) · item 83
- Salesperson/Customer, Support Staff/Customer and Person Away/Person Contacting ship with guidelines and criteria, off by default, as starting points the administrator can turn on and change.
- **Can show:** turn on Person Away/Person Contacting, ask to speak to the host, and hear the away message; turn on Salesperson/Customer as primary and ask what it can do.

## Milestone 13a: Identity and guidelines

| PR | Description | Status |
|---|---|---|
| [R92. Identity and goals in every reply](#r92-identity-and-goals-in-every-reply-medium--items-84-85) | The AI's identity and goals are sent with every reply. | Not started |
| [R93. Generating the default identity and guidelines](#r93-generating-the-default-identity-and-guidelines-medium--item-86) | The LLM drafts the default identity and guidelines. | Not started |
| [R94. Customization page](#r94-customization-page-medium--items-85-87) | A Customization page to edit the identity and guidelines. | Not started |
| [R95. Change history: compare, go back and edit](#r95-change-history-compare-go-back-and-edit-medium--item-88) | A history of customization changes to compare, go back to and edit. | Not started |

### R92. Identity and goals in every reply (Medium) · items 84, 85
- The identity (name, who it speaks as, purpose, who it is for, what it can help with), the goals and every context's guidelines are kept in `customization.json`, with a fixed default shipped for when the file is missing or can't be read (the error goes in the log). The first time this version starts, each context's guidelines move out of `contexts.json` into it, keyed by the context's id, edits included; from then on `customization.json` is their only copy, and `contexts.json` keeps name, kind, criteria, availability and priority. The Contexts page's guideline editor (R88), **Set back to how it shipped** and adding or deleting a context (R89) read and write them there. Each save writes the whole file to a temporary file and renames it over the old one, so a reader never sees half a save. What the file holds after the move is written as its first version. `GET /api/customization` returns all of it.
- Both are given to the LLM with every prompt, with the context guidelines. The file is read once per prompt, so a prompt never mixes two versions. "Who are you?", "What is your purpose?" and similar phrasings are a new Meta action that answers from the identity, so they are answered with general prompts off. The log has `Identity used` with the time taken.
- **Can show:** ask "Who are you?" and "What is your purpose?" with general prompts off and hear answers from the shipped identity, with Debug naming the Meta action; change the identity in the file and hear the next answer change; start with an edited Meta guideline in `contexts.json` and see it moved into `customization.json`, gone from `contexts.json`, and still used, with `Context guidelines moved` in the log.

### R93. Generating the default identity and guidelines (Medium) · item 86
- The first time the identity is needed, the chosen LLM drafts the identity and goals from what the app knows (the person's name and role if given, R24a to R24d; the topics in the content, R41f; the available contexts), and new guidelines for each context whose guidelines are still as shipped. Guidelines the administrator changed, and those of added contexts, are left alone.
- It runs in the background, never holding up a reply: the shipped defaults answer until it finishes. It has a 2 minute time limit *(default)*. On success it is saved as a new version and used from the next prompt, with `Customization generated` and the time in the log. On a timeout or failure the shipped defaults stay, with `Customization generation failed`, the reason and the time. If anything is saved while it runs, its result is dropped, with `Customization generation dropped` in the log. It runs once; after that, only **Generate again** (R94) runs it.
- **Can show:** with no saved identity, ask "Who are you?" at once and get the shipped answer without waiting, then ask again after generation and hear an identity that fits the default person and the content, with two versions in the file; with the LLM made to stall, the shipped defaults still answer, the log has the timeout after 2 minutes, and there is still one version.

### R94. Customization page (Medium) · items 85, 87
- A **Customization** page, headed Customization and reached from Settings in the host view, has an **Identity** section and a **Guidelines** section: the goals, and each context's guidelines in priority order. Each can be edited and saved, and takes effect from the next prompt.
- Editing guidelines moves here from the Contexts page (R88), which links to them; **Set back to how it shipped** stays for each context. They are already in `customization.json` (R92), so nothing moves and generated or edited guidelines are kept as they are. Saves are written as R92's are.
- **Generate again** runs R93's generation and shows the result as a draft beside the current text, with **Save** and **Discard**. Only **Save** keeps it.
- **Can show:** change the identity's name and a goal, save, ask "Who are you?" and hear the new name; change Meta's guidelines here and see the Contexts page link to them; Generate again and Discard, and see nothing change. The log has `Customization saved` and `Customization draft discarded`.

### R95. Change history: compare, go back and edit (Medium) · item 88
- Each version is a copy of the whole of `customization.json` (identity, goals and every context's guidelines), with when it was made and what made it (shipped, generated, saved or gone back to), kept in `customization.history/`, all of them *(default)*. A **History** button on the Customization page lists them, newest first.
- **Compare** shows the latest version beside the one before it, or any earlier version chosen from the list, with added and removed text marked. **Go back** to a version saves a copy of it as the new latest version, written as R94's saves are, so it can be undone. It changes only identity, goals and guidelines, never `contexts.json`; a context deleted since that version is left out, and a context added since keeps its current guidelines. **Edit** opens the latest version in the editor, and saving keeps the edit as a new version.
- **Can show:** change Meta's guidelines so its hints start with "Hint:", then go back to the version before, ask for a hint in a quiz and see the next reply follow the restored guidelines, with Meta's availability, priority and criteria unchanged. Make two edits and compare the latest with the one before, with the change marked; Generate again and Discard, then Save another draft, and see the history grow by one version, not two. The log has `Customization version saved` and `Customization restored`.

## Milestone 13b: Safe photos

| PR | Description | Status |
|---|---|---|
| [R123. Safe photo test](#r123-safe-photo-test-medium--items-102-103) | A test picking the models for safe photo checks. | Not started |
| [R124. Nudity check on upload](#r124-nudity-check-on-upload-medium--item-102) | Refuses a nude or sexual photo on upload. | Not started |
| [R125. Only a real person's face, head and shoulders](#r125-only-a-real-persons-face-head-and-shoulders-medium--items-103-104) | Refuses anything that isn't a real person's face, head and shoulders. | Not started |
| [R126. Safe photos everywhere](#r126-safe-photos-everywhere-simple--item-105) | The same safe photo checks on every way a photo comes in. | Not started |

### R123. Safe photo test (Medium) · items 102, 103
- An experiment, like R25a, picks the models before any app change. For nudity it compares NudeNet's detector (ONNX, about 12 MB, finds exposed body parts) with a safe/unsafe image classifier such as Falconsai's nsfw_image_detection (a ViT of about 330 MB), each only if its licence allows hosted use. For a real face it tries a small zero-shot image and text model (CLIP-style) asked whether the picture is a photograph of a person or a drawing, cartoon, painting, statue, doll, mask, animal or photo of a screen.
- The test set: Larry's photos and the photoreal default people, which must pass both checks; portraits in swimwear, sleeveless tops or with bare shoulders, which must pass the nudity check; the animated default people, paintings, cartoons, statues and pets, which must fail the real-face check. Unsafe test images come from a public research benchmark, are downloaded only when the test runs, and are never committed or shown; the PR shows only scores and counts.
- **Can show:** a table, for each model, of the share of safe photos wrongly refused and unsafe ones let through, the time per photo on 4 ARM cores, memory and model size, with the limits chosen and one model recommended for each check.

### R124. Nudity check on upload (Medium) · item 102
- R6's upload runs R123's chosen model on every image after the file type check and before conversion or storage. A photo found unsafe, or near the limit *(default)*, is deleted at once and refused with item 102's message. If the model is missing or fails, the photo is refused, never let through. The model loads at start-up with R8's, and is downloaded to `<IMAGESKIN_HOME>/models/safety` on first use.
- The log has `Photo safety checked` with the time taken, or `Photo refused: unsafe` with the score, never the image or its uploaded name, and `Safety check failed` with the reason.
- **Can show:** a portrait passes, with the time in the log; an unsafe test image from R123's set is refused and is not in `uploads/photos`; with the model file removed, a photo is refused with a plain message.

### R125. Only a real person's face, head and shoulders (Medium) · items 103, 104
- A new check beside R8's refuses a drawing, cartoon, painting, statue, doll, mask, animal or photo of a screen, and a face too small a share of the picture, each with its own plain message under the photo on the Setup page. The problems are saved in the photo's `.json` like R8's, and `FACE_CHECKS` goes up so earlier photos are checked again.
- Prepare crops the chosen photo to the head and shoulders, from the face landmarks, before the video engine sees it, so nothing below the shoulders can appear in the sample, idle or reply videos. The log has `Photo cropped to head and shoulders` with the time.
- **Can show:** a cartoon, a painting and a photo of a dog refused with their messages; a waist-up photo makes a head-and-shoulders sample video.

### R126. Safe photos everywhere (Simple) · item 105
- The same checks guard every other way a photo comes in: changing photos in Settings (R28), each customer's site (Milestone 20, which shares R6's upload) and, when it is built, photos taken on a phone (R111). The first time this version starts, every stored photo is checked once; one that fails is removed and the next best photo is used, or a default person if none is left, with `Stored photo refused` in the log. The packing script checks every default person once.
- **Can show:** an unsafe test photo stored before this version is gone after the next start, with the Setup page saying why; the packing script reports every default person checked.

### R135. What each page shows, and what it should (Simple) · items 115, 118
- A written review, like R123's test, before any page changes: for every page (home, Before you start, Setup, Avatar Choice and Your voice for the avatar, Chat, Manage Content, the wiki list and a wiki page, Settings, the recording guide, Goodbye and Page not found), a screenshot in the host and participant views, and each thing on it marked **keep**, **behind Show details**, **move** (to another page or a menu) or **remove**, with the reason. A small script counts each page's visible words, buttons and links, and sections, in both views, and saves the counts to `docs/ui-counts.json`.
- **Can show:** the review in the PR, with the counts for every page, and a list of the details that go behind Show details.

### R136. One Show details setting (Medium) · items 115, 116
- The Display group on the Settings page gets one **Show details** switch, off by default *(default)*, that takes in the timing metrics (R41a) and Debug's Copy chat, which become part of it. Details are shown by one shared `Details` wrapper, so each page PR only wraps what R135 marked. Off, technical words, ids, file names, sizes, model names, build steps and counts are not shown anywhere; they stay in the log.
- The log has `Show details changed` with the new value.
- **Can show:** with Show details off, the chat has no timing readout or Copy chat; on, both come back. A saved setting with metrics on turns into Show details on.

### R137. A simpler home page, header and short pages (Medium) · items 116, 117
- The home page has one main action (Start chatting, or Set up for a first-time user) and at most two others, each a short line. The header shows the page links the current view uses, at most five *(default)*, with the rest in a menu. Before you start, Goodbye and Page not found are each one short paragraph and one button.
- **Can show:** before and after screenshots of each page in both views, and their counts down from R135's.

### R138. A simpler Setup page (Medium) · items 116, 117
- The Setup page shows the steps one under the other, each closed to one line with a tick once done, and only the step in progress open. The checks show only what to fix; passed checks, scores and file details go behind Show details. Prepare shows one progress bar, the step in plain words and the time left (R134b), with the per-step list behind Show details. The review is the sample video and two buttons.
- **Can show:** a full setup with files, with before and after screenshots of each step and the page's counts down from R135's; with Show details on, the passed checks and the step list come back.

### R139. A simpler Avatar Choice (Medium) · items 116, 117
- Avatar Choice and Your voice for the avatar show the choices as pictures with short labels, one choice per row, and the faces as a grid; a choice already decided is not asked again. Download sizes and progress details go behind Show details, leaving one bar while the person is downloaded.
- **Can show:** pick a default person and start chatting, with before and after screenshots and the counts down from R135's.

### R140. A simpler chat page (Medium) · items 116, 117
- The video and the prompt box are the page; everything else is smaller or tucked away. Teach me, Quiz me and Assessment go into one **More** menu beside the prompt box *(default)*, topic hints show only before the first question and when the prompt box is empty, the history panel and the reply text open from one icon each, and the sound controls show when the video is pointed at or tapped.
- **Can show:** before and after screenshots in both views, with a question, a quiz and the history panel used from the new places, and the counts down from R135's.

### R141. A simpler Manage Content page (Medium) · items 116, 117
- Each PDF is one row: its name, one plain state (Ready, Being read, or a problem in a sentence) and a menu with Replace, Remove and Open in the wiki. Reading and wiki build details (pages, steps, times, sizes) go behind Show details. Adding a PDF is one button and dropping files on the page.
- **Can show:** add, replace and remove a PDF, with before and after screenshots and the counts down from R135's.

### R142. Simpler wiki pages (Medium) · items 116, 117
- The wiki list shows the topics and a search box. A wiki page shows its title, text and links to the PDF pages; Edit, History and Rebuild go into one menu in the host view, and the version and build details behind Show details.
- **Can show:** read a topic in the participant view and edit and restore one in the host view, with before and after screenshots and the counts down from R135's.

### R143. A simpler Settings page (Medium) · items 116, 117
- The Settings page is a short list of sections with plain names (View, Chat, Your avatar, The AI, Your data), each closed to one line saying its current choice and opened to change it. Each setting has one short line of help at most; the rest of the explanation is removed or behind Show details.
- **Can show:** change the view, the avatar and the LLM from the new sections, with before and after screenshots and the counts down from R135's.

### R144. A simpler recording guide (Medium) · items 116, 117
- The recording guide asks which laptop is used first and then shows only that laptop's steps, with the photo and voice parts as two short pages and the full script one click away. Questions and rare cases go into a closed **Problems?** part at the end.
- **Can show:** the guide for Windows 11 and for a Mac, before and after, and the counts down from R135's.

### R145. One look on every page, and the counts after (Medium) · items 117, 118
- A last pass over every page for one look: the same spacing, headings, buttons, messages and empty states, using shared styles rather than per-page ones, and anything R135 marked that a page PR left. R135's script runs again and saves the counts after beside the counts before.
- **Can show:** a table of every page's words, controls and sections before and after, each lower, and a contact sheet of every page in both views.

## Milestone 13d: Conversation history management

| PR | Description | Status |
|---|---|---|
| [R166. An id and a time for every exchange](#r166-an-id-and-a-time-for-every-exchange-simple--items-134-138) | Gives every exchange an id and a time. | Not started |
| [R167. The Conversation history page, with Save](#r167-the-conversation-history-page-with-save-medium--items-134-136) | A Conversation history page, with Save. | Not started |
| [R168. Load from computer](#r168-load-from-computer-medium--item-134) | Load a saved conversation from the computer. | Not started |
| [R169. Clear, with the choice to save first](#r169-clear-with-the-choice-to-save-first-simple--item-135) | Clear the conversation, with the choice to save first. | Not started |
| [R170. Deleting an exchange, and restoring or destroying it, on the server](#r170-deleting-an-exchange-and-restoring-or-destroying-it-on-the-server-medium--item-138) | Delete, restore or destroy an exchange on the server. | Not started |
| [R171. Delete, restore or destroy on the page](#r171-delete-restore-or-destroy-on-the-page-simple--item-138) | Delete, restore or destroy an exchange on the page. | Not started |
| [R172. Show deleted items](#r172-show-deleted-items-simple--item-139) | A Show deleted items checkbox. | Not started |
| [R173. Search for words or topics](#r173-search-for-words-or-topics-medium--item-137) | Search the conversation for words or topics. | Not started |
| [R174. Ask again](#r174-ask-again-simple--item-140) | Ask again: puts an earlier question back in the prompt box. | Not started |

In this milestone an **exchange** is one question with its reply.

### R166. An id and a time for every exchange (Simple) · items 134, 138
- Each turn of the conversation gets a lasting id and the time it was said, saved with it; a conversation saved before this gets ids and its file's time once, when it is first read. `GET /api/chat` gives them, so later PRs can name one exchange. Nothing on the page changes.
- The log has `Conversation ids added` with how many turns, once, for an older conversation.
- **Can show:** unit tests that ids are unique and kept across a restart, and that an older conversation is read with ids added.

### R167. The Conversation history page, with Save (Medium) · items 134, 136
- A **Conversation history** page (`#/history`), reached from **Manage history** at the top of the chat's open history and from Settings *(default)*, lists every exchange, newest first *(default)*, with its date and time, the question and the reply. At its top: **Save to computer**, **Load from computer** (R168), **Clear** (R169) and **Report**.
- **Save to computer** downloads the conversation as a file, `conversation-YYYY-MM-DD-HHMM.json`, with each exchange's id, time, question and reply; the deleted ones (R170) are left out *(default)*. **Report** opens a box that says only "Coming soon": it holds the place of a later report that helps the user analyze the conversation.
- The log has `Conversation saved` with how many exchanges and the time it took; the browser console logs `Conversation history shown` and `History report opened`.
- **Can show:** screenshots of the page and of Report's "Coming soon"; a saved file opened in a text editor.

### R168. Load from computer (Medium) · item 134
- **Load from computer** picks a file saved by R167 and adds its exchanges to the conversation, in order of their times; it never replaces the conversation, and an exchange already in it (by its id) or destroyed since (R170) is skipped, so loading the same file twice adds nothing. The summary sent to the LLM (item 15) is made again with them. A file that isn't a saved conversation is refused with a plain message and changes nothing.
- The log has `Conversation loaded` with how many exchanges were added and skipped, and `Conversation file refused` with why.
- **Can show:** a file loaded twice, its exchanges added once; a refused file's message; the log lines.

### R169. Clear, with the choice to save first (Simple) · item 135
- **Clear** asks first, in the app's confirmation box: "Clear the whole conversation?", "This can't be undone.", with **Save, then clear**, **Clear without saving** and **Keep it**, which has the focus so Enter or Escape never clears by mistake. Save, then clear saves as R167 does and clears only once the file has been made; if saving fails, nothing is cleared and the page says so. Settings' Clear conversation (R27b) opens the same box. Deleted exchanges are cleared too.
- The log has `Conversation cleared` with whether it was saved first, and `Clear conversation cancelled`.
- **Can show:** each of the three choices, and a save that fails leaving the conversation as it was.

### R170. Deleting an exchange, and restoring or destroying it, on the server (Medium) · item 138
- An exchange can be deleted, which hides it: it is no longer shown, sent to the LLM, saved, searched or counted, and the summary sent to the LLM is made again without it *(default)*. A deleted exchange can be restored, as it was, or destroyed: removed for good, its id kept without its words, so a saved file can never load it back (as item 35 does for the record).
- `POST /api/chat/exchanges/{id}/delete` and `/restore`, and `DELETE /api/chat/exchanges/{id}` to destroy, which only a deleted exchange can be. `GET /api/chat?deleted=true` gives the deleted ones too, marked.
- The log has `Exchange deleted`, `Exchange restored` and `Exchange destroyed` with its id, never its words.
- **Can show:** API tests of each step, that a deleted exchange isn't sent to the LLM, and that a destroyed one is skipped when a file holding it is loaded.

### R171. Delete, restore or destroy on the page (Simple) · item 138
- Each exchange on the Conversation history page has **Delete**. Deleted, it is replaced by one line, "Exchange deleted.", with **Restore** and **Destroy**; Destroy first asks "Destroy this exchange? It can't be brought back." The line goes when the page is left, and the exchange stays deleted.
- The browser console logs each choice.
- **Can show:** screenshots of an exchange deleted, restored, and destroyed after the confirmation.

### R172. Show deleted items (Simple) · item 139
- A **Show deleted items** checkbox at the top of the page, unticked each time the page opens *(default)*. Ticked, the deleted exchanges are shown in their place, greyed and marked deleted, each with **Restore**, **Destroy** (with R171's confirmation) and **Keep as is**, which leaves it deleted and closes its choices.
- The browser console logs `Deleted items shown` with how many.
- **Can show:** screenshots with the box unticked and ticked, and each of the three choices.

### R173. Search for words or topics (Medium) · item 137
- A search box at the top of the page finds words in the questions and replies, as R18's does in the chat (its code is shared), and a **Topic** list, of the content's topics (the wiki's first level, as R41f's "What can you talk about?" names them), or a topic typed in, shows only the exchanges about it, found with R39's lookup over each exchange's question and reply, best first. "N exchanges about …" says how many; clearing the search shows them all again. Deleted exchanges are searched only while Show deleted items is ticked.
- The log has `History topic search` with how many exchanges were found and the time it took.
- **Can show:** a word found in an old reply, a topic picked from the list and one typed in, each with its exchanges; unit tests of the topic search on a recorded conversation.

### R174. Ask again (Simple) · item 140
- Each exchange on the page has **Ask again**: it goes back to the chat with the same question in the prompt box, focused with the cursor at its end, so it can be edited first; nothing is sent until the user sends it.
- The browser console logs `Ask again` with the exchange's id.
- **Can show:** screenshots of Ask again filling the prompt box, a word changed, and the question sent.

## Milestone 14: Interaction history

| PR | Description | Status |
|---|---|---|
| [R48. Saving every interaction](#r48-saving-every-interaction-simple--item-34) | Saves every question and answer with its time and who asked. | Not started |
| [R49. My history, with deleting when allowed](#r49-my-history-with-deleting-when-allowed-medium--item-35) | A participant sees their own history, and can delete when allowed. | Not started |
| [R50. User history page](#r50-user-history-page-medium--item-36) | A host page with every question and answer, by date or participant. | Not started |

### R48. Saving every interaction (Simple) · item 34
- Every question and answer is saved with its date and time, and the participant's account or "anonymous".
- **Can show:** ask questions anonymously and as a participant, and see both saved in the logs and the store.

### R49. My history, with deleting when allowed (Medium) · item 35
- A signed-in participant sees their own questions and answers. A setting (off by default) lets participants delete a question with its answer; deleting removes it from the store for everyone (changed by Larry on 2026-10-17 from a soft clear that kept it for the administrator). The deleted turn's id is kept, without its words, so it can never come back, and a conversation summary that included it is rebuilt without it. The log notes the delete and who did it, not the words.
- **Can show:** with the setting off, a participant has no delete; turn it on, delete one question, and see it gone from the participant's history and from the store.

### R50. User history page (Medium) · item 36
- An administrator page with every prompt and answer, viewable by date and time, by participant, or anonymous only. The host can delete any question with its answer, removing it from the store, whatever the participant setting in R49 says.
- **Can show:** the administrator reviews a day's questions, then one participant's, then the anonymous ones.

## Milestone 15: Topics and understanding

| PR | Description | Status |
|---|---|---|
| [R53. Tagging questions with topics](#r53-tagging-questions-with-topics-medium--items-38-39) | Tags each question with its topics. | Not started |
| [R54. Rating the understanding a question shows](#r54-rating-the-understanding-a-question-shows-medium--item-40) | Rates the understanding each question shows. | Not started |

### R53. Tagging questions with topics (Medium) · items 38, 39
- Each new question keeps the wiki page ids the R39 lookup already found for it as its topics (none, one or several); there is no second classification. A one-off job runs the R39 lookup over the existing history, with progress like R12, and history loaded through R168 is tagged the same way. Tags are never rewritten when the wiki changes; a removed topic is shown as removed.
- **Can show:** ask a question on one topic, one spanning two topics and a general one, and see one, two and no topics in the store; then the backfill job's run time in the logs.

### R54. Rating the understanding a question shows (Medium) · item 40
- Each question from a signed-in participant is rated strong, weak or unrated for each of its topics, as defined in item 40 and using the method decided before this PR, with the reason kept. Existing questions are rated by the same job as R53. On the user history page (R50) the administrator can correct a rating; the correction is stored with the rating and reason, and is never overwritten by a re-rating.
- **Can show:** a question with a clear misconception rated weak, a plain fact-seeking question left unrated, and a two-topic question rated differently for each topic; then correct one rating and see it kept after the job runs again.

- An administrator page listing every topic with its question count and its counts of strong, weak and unrated questions (no single score), with topics nobody asked about listed separately.
- **Can show:** after a few questions on some topics, the page shows their counts and lists the untouched topics as not asked about.

### R58. Heads-up before deleting a question (Simple) · item 46
- The delete in R49 and R50 asks to confirm first, saying the question and its answer are removed from the record for everyone and can't be brought back (changed by Larry on 2026-10-17). It also checks that a deleted question leaves no trace in the topics, ratings, statistics, summaries and profile (R53 to R57).

## Milestone 19: Interests and personality

| PR | Description | Status |
|---|---|---|
| [R59. Working out interests and personality](#r59-working-out-interests-and-personality-medium--item-47) | Works out each participant's interests and how they respond. | Not started |

### R59. Working out interests and personality (Medium) · item 47
- For each signed-in participant, a job reads their questions, the answers and their next message after each answer, and keeps a short profile: interests, and how they respond to answers, each with its one-line evidence and the questions it came from. Uses the method decided before this PR. Runs per the R59 decision and keeps the previous profile until a new one is ready; anonymous questions are not used. Participants with too few questions get no profile.
- **Can show:** a participant who keeps asking for real-world examples and follows up with "why" gets interests and a response style that cite those questions; a participant with two questions gets none; the job's run time per participant in the logs.

### R60. Profile on the participant summary page, with corrections (Simple) · item 47
- Adds the profile from R59 to the R57 participant summary page, under the topics, labelled as an impression drawn from their questions. Each line shows its evidence and links to its questions. The administrator can edit or remove a line; the change is kept and never overwritten when the profile is worked out again.
- **Can show:** open a participant's summary, see their interests and response style with evidence, remove one line, rerun R59 and see it stay removed.

## Milestone 22: Gathering information from visitors and tickets

| PR | Description | Status |
|---|---|---|
| [R79. Gathering feedback in conversation](#r79-gathering-feedback-in-conversation-medium--item-73) | Notices feedback in conversation and passes it on when the visitor agrees. | Not started |
| [R80. Collecting contact information with consent](#r80-collecting-contact-information-with-consent-medium--item-74) | Collects a name and email with consent. | Not started |
| [R81. Opening a ticket from a conversation](#r81-opening-a-ticket-from-a-conversation-medium--item-75) | Drafts and opens a ticket from a conversation. | Not started |
| [R82. Answers that use open tickets](#r82-answers-that-use-open-tickets-medium--items-70-76) | Answers on the support site that use open tickets. | Not started |
| [R83. Asking about what was gathered](#r83-asking-about-what-was-gathered-medium--item-76) | A page to ask the AI about the feedback, contacts and tickets gathered. | Not started |

### R79. Gathering feedback in conversation (Medium) · item 73
- After a reply, feedback in what the visitor said is noticed (by the method decided before this PR); the AI asks whether to pass it on and, if they agree, saves it as feedback with its conversation. A site setting turns this on for a customer's site, and adds a feedback page for its customer showing only that site's feedback.
- **Can show:** on site A, say "this answer was confusing", agree to pass it on, and find it on site A's feedback page linked to the conversation, and not on site B's or the support staff's; decline once and see nothing saved.

### R80. Collecting contact information with consent (Medium) · item 74
- When a reply, ticket or follow-up needs it, the AI asks for a name and email, saying why, and saves them only if the visitor agrees. The site's customer or support staff see them on a contacts page and can delete them; deleting also blanks the name and email out of the saved conversation, any feedback or ticket made from it, and the search index.
- **Can show:** on site A, ask for a follow-up, give an email, find it on site A's contacts page and not on site B's, then delete it, and see that a search, or a question on R83's page once it exists, no longer finds the email in the original conversation.

### R81. Opening a ticket from a conversation (Medium) · item 75
- When the AI can't solve a problem, it drafts a ticket from the problem alone, with no contact details or unrelated conversation, and opens it once the visitor has approved it, editing it if they wish. On the support site the visitor first sees the public warning and the ticket joins the public list. On a customer's site the ticket is private to that site: a tickets page lets its customer change the status and reply, and the opener answers through their private link, with the confirmed email updates of R77.
- **Can show:** give an email, then describe a problem the guide doesn't cover; check that the drafted ticket holds neither the email nor the rest of the conversation, approve it, and find it in the public list. Do the same on site A and find the ticket on site A's tickets page only, not in the public list or on site B; the customer asks a follow-up, the opener gets the email and answers through their link, and no one without it can read or answer the ticket.

### R82. Answers that use open tickets (Medium) · items 70, 76
- On the support site, the lookup (R39) also searches the public tickets; a customer's site never answers visitors from its private tickets. Visitors' ticket text is passed to the LLM as unconfirmed reports, marked as data and never as instructions; only staff status and replies count as confirmed. An answer about a known problem says whether it is reported or confirmed by support, with the ticket linked.
- **Can show:** with a visitor's open ticket about a login problem, ask about logging in and hear that it has been reported; after staff confirm it, hear that support has confirmed it. A ticket whose text tries to give the AI instructions is quoted as a report, not obeyed.

### R83. Asking about what was gathered (Medium) · item 76
- A page for a site's host or the support staff to ask the AI about the feedback, contacts, conversations and tickets gathered for that site only, with each answer linking to its sources.
- **Can show:** ask "Summarize the open tickets about subdomains" and get a summary linking to each ticket, with the time taken in the logs; on site A, ask about site B's feedback and get nothing from it.

## Milestone 23: Handling disputes

| PR | Description | Status |
|---|---|---|
| [R101. Admin role and admin sign-in](#r101-admin-role-and-admin-sign-in-medium--item-89) | An admin role for disputes and messages across every site. | Not started |
| [R102. Messages and the notification mark](#r102-messages-and-the-notification-mark-medium--item-90) | Messages between the admin and users, with an unread mark. | Not started |
| [R103. Contact admin](#r103-contact-admin-simple--item-90) | A Contact admin link for anyone. | Not started |
| [R104. Dispute link and form](#r104-dispute-link-and-form-medium--item-91) | A link and form to dispute an image or voice. | Not started |
| [R105. Verifying the disputer's email or phone](#r105-verifying-the-disputers-email-or-phone-medium--item-92) | Verifies the disputer's email or phone with a code. | Not started |
| [R106. A dispute turns off the image or voice](#r106-a-dispute-turns-off-the-image-or-voice-medium--items-91-93) | A verified dispute turns off the disputed image or voice. | Not started |
| [R107. Disputing the dispute: the admin's dispute page](#r107-disputing-the-dispute-the-admins-dispute-page-medium--item-94) | The admin's dispute page, to turn an image or voice back on. | Not started |
| [R108. Cancelling a dispute](#r108-cancelling-a-dispute-simple--item-95) | Cancelling a dispute. | Not started |

### R101. Admin role and admin sign-in (Medium) · item 89
- A third role beside host and participant: the admin, who signs in as Milestone 27 built (R149, R150), reaches every site, but only for disputes and messages, never a site's settings, content or history. Hosts and participants never get the admin's pages.
- **Can show:** sign in as the admin and see the disputes and messages pages in the admin view; a host and a participant are refused them; the admin opens no site's settings. The log has `Admin signed in`.

### R102. Messages and the notification mark (Medium) · item 90
- The admin sends a short text message to a host or a signed-in participant, who reads it in an inbox and can reply. While any message is unread, a mark with the number unread shows on every page; reading it clears the mark.
- **Can show:** the admin messages a host, the host sees the mark on the chat and settings pages, opens the inbox, replies, and the mark goes; the admin sees the reply. The log has `Message sent` with who sent it and to whom, not the words.

### R103. Contact admin (Simple) · item 90
- A **Contact admin** link for any host or participant, at any time, sends the admin a message about any issue; an anonymous visitor gives an email or phone for the reply *(default)*.
- **Can show:** a participant and an anonymous visitor each send a message from the link, and both reach the admin's inbox, the visitor's with their contact detail.

### R104. Dispute link and form (Medium) · item 91
- A clear **Dispute this image or voice** link wherever the person is shown, for participants and visitors. The form asks what is disputed (image, voice or both), an explanation of why the host does not have the right to it, and an email and a phone number, or a reason for each one that is not available. Every field is required, and it is checked for nonsense as decided before this PR. On a default person's picture or voice the link says it is of a made-up person or a licensed voice and offers **Contact admin** instead.
- The dispute is saved as waiting for verification (R105); nothing is turned off yet.
- **Can show:** a form missing a field, or with an explanation of random letters, is refused with a plain message; a complete one is saved as waiting; on a default person the link offers Contact admin. The log has `Dispute received` with the site and what is disputed.

### R105. Verifying the disputer's email or phone (Medium) · item 92
- A code is sent to the disputer's email or phone, as decided before this PR; entering it verifies them, and only then does the dispute go through. The form says plainly that if this doesn't work for them they can message the admin directly with the full details, and must then wait for the admin to reply.
- **Can show:** a wrong code is refused and the dispute stays waiting; the right one verifies it; the form's note links to Contact admin. The log has `Dispute verified` with how it was verified.

### R106. A dispute turns off the image or voice (Medium) · items 91, 93
- Once a dispute is verified, the disputed custom image or voice, or both, is turned off on that site at once, and the site falls back to the default avatar decided before this PR, without deleting the host's files. The admin and the site's host get a message (R102) with the dispute's details; the host's says how to dispute it (R107).
- **Can show:** verify a dispute of a site's custom photo and see the next reply spoken by the default person, with the host's photo still on disk; dispute only the voice and hear the photo speak in a ready-made voice. Both the admin and the host see the notification mark. The log has `Dispute applied` with the site, what was turned off, and the time taken.

### R107. Disputing the dispute: the admin's dispute page (Medium) · item 94
- The host disputes it by messaging the admin, offline, from the dispute message. An admin page lists every dispute with its status (waiting, applied, cancelled, kept off, restored), its details and its messages. The admin can **Turn back on** the image or voice, or **Keep it off**, and the host and the disputer are told by message (or by email for an anonymous disputer) *(default)*.
- **Can show:** the host replies to the dispute message, the admin reads it on the dispute page, turns the photo back on and sees the site speak with it again, and both the host and the disputer are told. The log has `Dispute decided` with the decision.

### R108. Cancelling a dispute (Simple) · item 95
- A disputer cancels from **Cancel a dispute** beside the dispute link. A participant who disputed while signed in must sign in to cancel; an anonymous visitor enters the same phone number and email again, or "NA" for whichever they left out. Cancelling turns the image or voice back on, unless the admin has already decided, and tells the admin and the host.
- **Can show:** an anonymous visitor cancels with their email and "NA" for the phone and sees the photo back; a wrong email is refused; a signed-in participant is asked to sign in first. The log has `Dispute cancelled`.

## Milestone 24: Phones in the browser

| PR | Description | Status |
|---|---|---|
| [R109. A phone-sized screen](#r109-a-phone-sized-screen-medium--item-96) | Every page fits a phone held upright. | Not started |
| [R110. Video and sound on phones](#r110-video-and-sound-on-phones-medium--items-11-17-96) | Replies and the idle video play with sound on phones. | Not started |
| [R111. Photos and recordings from the phone](#r111-photos-and-recordings-from-the-phone-medium--items-1-96) | Take a photo and record the voice on a phone. | Not started |
| [R112. Hold to talk on phones](#r112-hold-to-talk-on-phones-simple--items-9-96) | Hold to talk on phones. | Not started |
| [R113. Add to the home screen](#r113-add-to-the-home-screen-simple--item-96) | Add the site to the phone's home screen. | Not started |

**The approach: phones are clients of the hosted server** *(default)*. Everything heavy stays where it runs today, on the hosted server at app.me0.ai and the customers' sites (R32, R71): llama.cpp's LLM, Kokoro and Chatterbox for the voice, and LivePortrait for the video, which the server renders sentence by sentence and streams to the screen (R21, R22d). A phone only does what the browser does now: it uploads photos and recordings, sends prompts and plays the clips. Rendering on the phone was ruled out, since preparing a face takes about 17 minutes on 4 server cores and the voice clone takes 1.5 to 2 seconds per second of speech on a CPU, which a phone could not do at reply speed or without draining its battery. So the first step is the web app itself working well in a phone's browser (this milestone), which every later step reuses; then the apps wrap the same Preact build with Capacitor (Milestone 25), so there is still one codebase for the screens, and the stores come last (Milestone 26).

### R109. A phone-sized screen (Medium) · item 96
- Every page fits a phone held upright, from 360 to 430 pixels wide: the video above the chat and prompt box, menus and the history panel that open over the page, buttons large enough to tap, and the prompt box above the on-screen keyboard. The desktop layout doesn't change.
- **Can show:** screenshots of setup, chat, settings and Manage Content at iPhone and Pixel sizes in the browser's device mode, with nothing cut off or scrolling sideways; the existing desktop screenshots unchanged.

### R110. Video and sound on phones (Medium) · items 11, 17, 96
- Replies, the idle video and the sample video play in place, not full screen, in Safari on iOS and Chrome on Android. Where a phone won't play sound before a tap, the first tap anywhere starts it, and a reply that arrives first waits rather than playing silent. Clips still join without a gap (R23). The server sends each clip in a form both phones play (H.264 and AAC in MP4).
- **Can show:** on an iPhone and an Android phone, ask a question and see and hear the reply start within the usual time (the timing readout of R22a), with the idle video between replies; the log has the phone's browser with each reply's timings.

### R111. Photos and recordings from the phone (Medium) · items 1, 96
- On the upload screen, **Take a photo** opens the phone's camera and **Record** records the voice in the browser, as well as picking files. Both reuse the setup wizard's camera and recording steps (R132, R133), sized for a phone held upright. Recordings in the phone's own format (AAC in MP4 on iOS, Opus on Android) are converted like any upload (R2) and pass the same sound checks (R10, R11).
- **Can show:** on a phone, take a photo and make a recording, see both pass their checks, and prepare and review as on a computer. The log has each upload's format and the time to convert it.

### R112. Hold to talk on phones (Simple) · items 9, 96
- Hold to talk (R24) works with a finger held on the button, in browsers that offer speech recognition, and is hidden where they don't, leaving the prompt box. The screen doesn't scroll or select text while it is held.
- **Can show:** on Chrome on Android and Safari on iOS, hold, speak and see the prompt sent; in a browser without recognition the button is not shown.

### R113. Add to the home screen (Simple) · item 96
- A web app manifest and icons, so the site can be added to the phone's home screen and opens full screen, without the browser's bars, with the app's name and icon. Nothing is kept offline: with no connection it says it needs one.
- **Can show:** add the site to the home screen on both phones and open it from the icon, full screen; with flight mode on, see the plain message.

## Milestone 25: iOS and Android apps

| PR | Description | Status |
|---|---|---|
| [R114. The app shell](#r114-the-app-shell-medium--item-97) | The iOS and Android app shell with Capacitor. | Not started |
| [R115. Signing in from an app: the server](#r115-signing-in-from-an-app-the-server-medium--item-98) | The server side of signing in from an app. | Not started |
| [R116. Signing in from an app: the screens](#r116-signing-in-from-an-app-the-screens-simple--item-98) | The app's sign-in screens. | Not started |
| [R117. Microphone, camera and speech in the apps](#r117-microphone-camera-and-speech-in-the-apps-medium--items-9-99) | The microphone, camera and speech recognition in the apps. | Not started |
| [R118. Behaving like a phone app](#r118-behaving-like-a-phone-app-simple--item-100) | The apps behave like phone apps: sound, screen and calls. | Not started |
| [R119. The apps built on every change](#r119-the-apps-built-on-every-change-medium--item-97) | CI builds both apps on every change. | Not started |

### R114. The app shell (Medium) · item 97
- A `mobile/` folder with Capacitor's iOS and Android projects, which bundle the web build (R5) and open it as the app, with the app's name and icon. The address of the site it connects to is set when it is built (app.me0.ai by default), and every request goes to it over HTTPS.
- **Can show:** the app built and opened in the iOS Simulator and the Android Emulator, showing the first screen of the hosted site's app; the build commands and their output in the PR. The app logs its version and the site it connects to when it starts.

### R115. Signing in from an app: the server (Medium) · item 98
- The server lets the apps in: it allows requests from the apps' own origins, and a sign-in, as decided before this PR, returns a token that the app sends with every request instead of the browser's password prompt or cookie. Tokens last until signed out *(default)* and can be revoked.
- **Can show:** unit tests for signing in, using, expiring and revoking a token, and for refusing another origin; the log has `App signed in` with the site and role, never the token.

### R116. Signing in from an app: the screens (Simple) · item 98
- The first time it opens, the app asks for the site (a subdomain of me0.ai), then the host's or participant's sign-in. The token is kept in the iPhone's Keychain or Android's Keystore, so the app opens straight to the chat after that. **Sign out** in Settings forgets it.
- **Can show:** in both simulators, sign in to a site, close and open the app and land on the chat, then sign out and see the sign-in again.

### R117. Microphone, camera and speech in the apps (Medium) · items 9, 99
- The apps ask for the microphone, the camera and speech recognition with a plain reason for each, only when first used. Hold to talk uses the speech recognition decided before this PR, and Take a photo and Record (R111) use the phone's own. If one is refused, the app says how to allow it in the phone's settings and keeps working without it.
- **Can show:** on a phone, allow each one and use it; refuse the microphone and see the note and the prompt box still working.

### R118. Behaving like a phone app (Simple) · item 100
- Replies play aloud even with the iPhone's silent switch on, like a video app *(default)*; the screen stays on while the person speaks; the reply stops when the app goes to the background or a call comes in, and the idle video comes back when it returns. The Android back button goes back a page, and leaves only from the chat.
- **Can show:** on a phone, with the silent switch on, hear a reply; switch apps mid-reply and back, and see it stopped and the person idling. The log has `App paused` and `App resumed`.

### R119. The apps built on every change (Medium) · item 97
- CI builds the Android app on every pull request, and the iOS app on a macOS runner, unsigned, with the web tests, so a change that breaks either app's build fails the PR.
- **Can show:** a CI run with both builds passing, and one where a broken native setting fails it.

## Milestone 26: In the App Store and Google Play

| PR | Description | Status |
|---|---|---|
| [R120. What the stores require](#r120-what-the-stores-require-medium--item-101) | The privacy policy and other things the stores require. | Not started |
| [R121. Signed builds for testing](#r121-signed-builds-for-testing-medium--item-101) | Signed builds for TestFlight and Play internal testing. | Not started |

### R120. What the stores require (Medium) · item 101
- Before sending the apps for review: a privacy policy page on the site, Apple's privacy manifest and Google Play's data safety answers for the photos, recordings and conversations sent to the server, the age rating, and, inside the app, **Delete my data** (R31), deleting the account, **Dispute this image or voice** and **Contact admin** (Milestone 23), which the stores ask of apps that make content from people's faces and voices.
- **Can show:** the privacy page on the site; in the app, each of those links working; the manifest and data safety answers in the PR.

### R121. Signed builds for testing (Medium) · item 101
- A tagged release builds signed apps in CI, with the signing keys kept as repository secrets, and sends them to TestFlight and Google Play's internal testing, where Larry installs them.
- **Can show:** a tagged release reaching TestFlight and Play internal testing, and the app installed from each on a phone; no key in the repository or the log.

### R122. The store listings (Simple) · item 101
- Each store's listing: the name, description, screenshots from R109 to R118, and the review notes, with a test site and sign-in that starts with a default person (Milestone 10a). Memberships are handled as decided before this PR. Then both are sent for review.
- **Can show:** both listings sent for review, and, once approved, the app found in each store.

After R122, every item in features.md is covered by a PR, R61 to R78, R84 to R95 and R123 to R126 having been built after R47. The PRs marked in [After the engine decision](#after-the-engine-decision) are redefined when each one starts, and the count may change by a PR or two.

## Optional, not counted

- **Second video engine** (the other of local or hosted), added behind R4's interface. Medium, 1 PR. features.md calls for it "later"; the app is complete without it.
- **Fine-tuned voice** from all the recordings, for a closer likeness. 2 or more Medium PRs.
- **whisper.cpp speech-to-text** on the server, if browser speech recognition isn't good enough. Medium, 1 PR.
- **Push notifications** on the phone for an unread message (R102) or a dispute (R106), through Apple's and Google's push services. Medium, 1 PR.
- **Rendering on the phone**, if phones become fast enough to clone the voice and animate the face at reply speed, so the apps can work without the server. Several Large PRs; not planned.

## About this plan

- **The order.** A talking photoreal sample video comes first, then setup in the browser, then proof that the person's own voice can be cloned, before any chat work, because the project fails if the voice doesn't work (Larry, 2026-10-07). The chat, real-time replies, PDF content and answers from it, a professional look, the default people, settings, choice of LLM and hosting follow, then speed-ups, bug fixing, an admin view, a setup wizard, host login and the resource view, many customers on me0.ai, accounts, support, thinking and the quality of responses, contexts, the identity, safe photos, a simpler UI, managing the conversation history, history and participant insights, disputes, and phones last. Larry has moved several milestones since they were added; each milestone's section says when it was added and why it sits where it does.
- **Build order.** PRs are built in the order they appear in this file, not in number order.
- **Numbering.** PRs are numbered R1 to R193 so they don't get mixed up with GitHub PR numbers. A PR added later beside an existing one gets a letter, such as R4a, R22b or R24c2, so the other numbers stay the same.
- **Items.** "item 6" and the like refer to [features.md](features.md).
